**Added 2026-11-25, 20:45, before any run: the shin touches the foot's contact margin on MPM sand and stops the
ankle; rerun without that contact (asked by the lead after check 3 of T5).**

- *The finding, and what I verified myself before writing this.* In the MPM tasks the collision shapes of the
  two feet carry a contact margin of 2 cm (`FOOT_CONTACT_MARGIN = 0.02`, set on `*_ankle_roll_link` by the
  robot's `collision_props` in `velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/streaming_scene_cfg.py`). The sand needs
  it, but the rigid contact between the robot's own links reads the same margin, so the shin (`knee_link`)
  meets the foot 2 cm early and the ankle pitch joint cannot go beyond about −0.46 rad, 26 of its 50 degrees
  toes-up. The rigid and the RFT tasks have no such margin. In my own rollouts on MPM sand (slope tile and
  planar ramp at +30°, planar +10°, level beach sand; three policies) no ankle sample is below −0.51 rad, the
  1st percentile is −0.44 to −0.48 rad in every cell, and 33 to 93 % of the samples lie between −0.50 and
  −0.40 rad. The checker's local trials (3 per cell, +30°: MPM 0 of 3 without the contact, 3 of 3 with it; RFT
  2 of 3 both ways) are not results. I have seen them; this reading is written knowing them.
- *What is changed, in the evaluation only.* A new option of `eval_walk.py`, off by default: when the model is
  built, the shape pairs (`knee_link` shapes, `ankle_roll_link` shapes) of each leg of each robot are added to
  the collision filter (`builder.add_shape_collision_filter_pair`). Nothing else changes: the margin stays for
  the sand and for every other contact; the task code is not touched. Each run records how many pairs were
  filtered (2 per robot expected) and the ankle pitch reached. No policy is retrained.
- *Cells.* MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp`; beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed
  setting, footprint tracker on. Planar ramp with the level approach at +10°, +20°, +30° and the slope tile at
  +20°, +30°: 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 48, five robots per run). Level ground on beach sand and on loose
  poppy seed: 10 trials per cell (seeds 41, 42). Every cell is run twice in one workflow, on the same GPU
  type: with the contact as it is (control) and with the pair filtered.
- *Does the filter act?* In the filtered arm the 1st percentile of the ankle pitch has to lie below −0.50 rad
  for a policy; if it does not, that cell tests nothing for that policy and is reported so.
- *Reading per policy and cell, fixed now.* Goals with the contact against goals without it: "the shin-foot
  contact changes the outcome" if Fisher's exact test gives p below 0.01, otherwise "no change shown", with the
  difference in points and its 95 % interval (Newcombe). Reported beside it: kinds of failure, velocity error
  of the trials that reach the goal, ankle pitch (1st, 5th percentile, median), the sole's angle to the
  ground in loaded stance, sand motion under the sole.
- *Reading for the comparison of the policies, fixed now.* In the filtered arm, MPM against RFT per cell by the
  reading of the entry of 02:54 (success: Fisher's exact test at 0.01; velocity error: 0.05 m/s and two
  pooled standard deviations where both reach 10 goals). *"The separation of MPM from RFT at +30° holds without
  the shin-foot contact"* if MPM is better at +30° on the planar ramp and on the tile in the filtered arm.
  *"The separation at +30° was the shin-foot contact"* if in the filtered arm MPM is not better than RFT at +30°
  on both terrains and the contact changes the MPM policy's outcome there. Anything else is reported terrain
  by terrain. *MJWarp:* "its failure on this ground was the shin-foot contact" for a cell in which the contact
  changes its outcome upward; "not the contact" for a cell with at most 3 goals in both arms; other cells are
  given with their numbers.
- *What a result would and would not mean.* The MPM policy was trained with this contact and the baselines
  without it, so the filtered arm is the fairer test of the baselines and an unfair one of the MPM policy,
  which may rest on a stop that is no longer there. If the MPM policy fails without the contact, that shows
  that this policy as trained needs it; a comparison of training methods then needs an MPM policy trained
  without the contact, which is the user's decision and is not started here. If nothing changes, the merged
  comparisons stand as they are.

**Added 2026-11-25, 22:26, a plan, not run and not to be run before the lead says so: does the fixed code act
like the arm without the shin-foot contact? (asked by the lead after the user's decision)** The user decided the
fix: the feet's 2 cm contact margin is given to the MPM solver's collider only and the rigid margin of the feet
becomes 0; rss-T2 implements it in the unified worktree. The fix and my evaluation option are not the same
change: the option removes one contact pair (shin against foot) and leaves 2 cm on every other rigid contact of
the foot (the rigid base under the sand, rigid tiles, the other foot); the fix removes the 2 cm from all of them.
They should agree where the foot touches nothing rigid but the shin.

- *Cell.* Slope tile +30°, beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed setting, footprint tracker on, the three
  policies as they are (MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp`; no new policy), seeds 41 to 48, five
  robots per run, 40 trials per policy; a new code bundle made from the worktree after the fix, run without the
  option; 8 workflows of one task, on the same GPU type as the arm it is compared with. This is the cell with
  the most room to move in both directions (without the contact: MJWarp 0, RFT 28, MPM 1 of 40).
- *Compared with.* The arm without the contact of the same cell on bundle `300131cc8bc652ef` (the 8 workflows
  `t3-shin-t30-s41…48-1`, tasks `beach-sand-p30-noshin`). Nothing is rerun on the old bundle.
- *Reading, fixed now.* Per policy: "the fixed code acts like the arm without the contact" if (a) the goals of
  40 do not differ by Fisher's exact test at 0.01, (b) the 1st percentile of the ankle pitch lies below
  −0.50 rad, and (c) the first recorded pelvis position and joint angles of every trial equal those of the
  compared arm. "The fixed code differs" for a policy with p below 0.01; the difference is then looked for in
  the foot's other rigid contacts, starting with the share of samples in which a foot touches the rigid base.
  If (b) fails, the stop is still there and the fix is not in the bundle. The difference is always given in
  points with its 95 % interval (Newcombe): with 40 trials per arm only differences of roughly 25 to 30 points
  can be told apart, so "no change shown" is not "identical"; reruns of the same start part after about a
  second, so equal outcomes trial by trial are not expected.
- *What it is not.* It does not test the new MPM training; it shows only that the evaluation of the three
  existing policies on the fixed code reproduces the arm without the contact, so that the remaining cells of
  that arm (run on bundle `300131cc8bc652ef` with the option) can be read as "the fixed simulation".
- *Kept apart.* All shin-foot waves in flight stay on bundle `300131cc8bc652ef`: their control arm needs the
  stop, and a bundle made after the fix no longer has it.

**Added 2026-11-25, 23:11, before the level-ground cells are read (they are not submitted yet): does the MPM
policy lean forward without the shin-foot stop? (asked by the lead for the user)** The user's hypothesis for the
hardware: the MPM policy leaned forward on the real robot because the simulated shin-foot stop it rested on is
absent there. No new runs: the level cells of the entry of 20:45 (level beach sand and loose poppy seed, 0.50 m
bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed setting, 10 trials per policy and arm, seeds 41 and 42, five robots per run, both arms
in one workflow, bundle `300131cc8bc652ef`) are read for it. Known when this is written: at +30° without the
contact the MPM policy falls forward (twelfth check) and in the two clips it leans far forward; T2's check on
level beach sand found ankle, foot force and step frequency of the fixed code equal to my filtered arm and did
not measure the lean. No level cell of mine with and without the contact exists yet.

- *Quantities, per policy and arm, beside the goals.* (1) Forward lean of the torso [deg]: the angle between the
  torso link's up axis and the vertical, in the vertical plane of the walking direction, positive forward; the
  torso's orientation is the recorded pelvis orientation turned by the three recorded waist joint angles (yaw,
  roll, pitch, in the order of the joint chain). Given as the mean over trials of each trial's mean, and the
  5th to 95th percentile over all samples. (2) The same for the pelvis alone. (3) Pelvis height above the
  settled sand surface under it, mean [m]. (4) Forward speed over the command (1.0 m/s), mean. (5) Ankle
  pitch, median [rad].
- *Window ("steady walking").* Each trial from 1.5 s after the start to its end; for a trial that falls, the
  last 0.5 s before the fall are left out. A trial with less than 1 s inside the window is left out and
  counted; the number of trials used is given with every value.
- *Reading for the MPM policy, per sand, fixed now.* Difference = mean torso lean without the contact minus
  mean torso lean with it, over trials, with its 95 % interval (Welch's t over the trial means). Spread of the
  control arm = the larger of (i) the difference between the control arm's two seed means and (ii) two
  standard deviations of its trial means. *"The forward lean appears only without the stop"* if the difference
  is positive and larger than that spread. *"The lean is smaller without the stop"* if it is negative and
  larger in size than the spread. Otherwise *"no lean difference shown"*. The statement is made for both sands
  together only if both give it; otherwise sand by sand. The same numbers are given for RFT and MJWarp without
  a rule.
- *What a result would and would not mean.* It can show whether removing the stop makes the simulated MPM
  policy carry its torso further forward on level sand. It cannot show that this is why the real robot leaned:
  the real robot's lean is not measured here, and the simulated robot without the stop still differs from the
  real one in other ways (the sim-to-sim gate of T6 and the hardware log are the places for that). If the MPM
  policy does not reach steady walking without the contact on a sand, that is reported in place of a lean.

and MPM's 26° and 28° toes-down sole at +20° to the stop. No new cells: the arms with and without the contact

  the means of two of the three quantities when the cells were joined. Sole angle in mid-stance at +20°, with →
  without the contact (tile; planar): MJWarp −57.4 → −43.3; −56.2 → −45.2°. RFT −26.3 → −23.0; −27.3 → −23.5°.
  MPM −28.2 → −35.1; −28.6 → −36.8°. Sand under the sole per stance: MJWarp 5.6 → 6.3; 5.2 → 5.3 cm. RFT
  3.5 → 3.5; 3.8 → 3.8 cm. MPM 2.2 → 4.8; 2.4 → 5.0 cm. At +30° (tile; planar), sole angle: MJWarp −69.1 →

- *Quantities, per policy, cell and arm.* (1) Sole angle to the slope surface in loaded stance [deg], toes up
  positive: per stance the median over mid-stance (from the end of dig-in to two samples before lift-off), per

  that foot carries more than 20 % of body weight, on the slope. (3) Sand under the sole per stance [cm]: the
  downhill motion of the sand that lay within 3 cm under the sole, after dig-in, per trial the mean over its
  stances. A trial needs at least three stances on the slope; failed trials count up to the fall. Given as
  mean and standard deviation over trials, with the number of trials used.

  between seeds of the control arm = the largest difference between the means of its seeds (8 seeds of five

  its 95 % interval (Welch over trial means).

(twelfth check), so the shin-foot pair is listed as a candidate; T2 measures no force on it in the tow task. I

**Added 2026-11-26, 04:13, before any checkpoint of the retrained MPM policy `j0scsgdy` is evaluated: two counts
from now on, the goal and the walking goal (asked by the lead after the fourteenth check).** The goal rule of
every table so far is "the pelvis passes the goal line without a termination". The fourteenth check showed that
it counts a fall through the line as a goal (MJWarp's 26 goals at planar +15° without the stop and its 9 at
+10° with it are all of that kind) and that the count then depends on where the line is. I have seen the
check's numbers and its table of what a stricter count would give in the cells that exist; the criterion below
is the one the check proposes, chosen after those data, and it is fixed here before the retrained policy has
any evaluation.

- *No rescoring.* The cells merged so far keep their counts by the existing rule; where the check's stricter
  count differs it is quoted from the check and named as such.
- *From the first evaluation of `j0scsgdy` on, every table carries two counts per policy and cell:* the
  **goal** by the existing rule, and the **walking goal**.
- *Walking goal, exactly.* A trial that is a goal by the existing rule and in which, over the window of 2.0 s
  that ends with the sample in which the goal is scored (the trial's last sample by its recorded duration),
  both hold: (a) *speed:* the mean of the pelvis's forward velocity (world velocity along the walking
  direction, +X) over the window, divided by the mean commanded forward speed over the same window, lies
  between 0.7 and 1.3; (b) *not sinking:* the median pelvis height over the last 0.5 s of the window is at
  most 0.05 m below its median over the first 0.5 s of the window (2.0 to 1.5 s before the goal sample);
  pelvis height is the pelvis's height above the terrain surface vertically under it, as recorded.
- *A trial that ends early.* If fewer than 2.0 s are recorded before the goal sample, the window is all that
  is recorded, and the two half-second spans are its first and last 0.5 s; with less than 1.0 s recorded the
  trial is not a walking goal. (At 1.0 m/s the goal line of the present cells is reached after 5 s or more,
  so this case is not expected.)
- *After the line, for new runs.* Where the terrain and the sand bed allow, new evaluation runs keep the goal
  line where it is and record 2.0 s and at least 2 m of sand beyond it (today the bed ends 0.3 m behind the
  line); a walking goal then also requires that no termination occurs in those 2.0 s. If a cell cannot be
  given that length, this part is left out for that cell and the table says so. The change is made in the
  evaluation script only and is tested on one cell of the baselines before it is used.
- *How comparisons are read.* Every comparison of policies (success: Fisher's exact test at 0.01 with the
  difference and its 95 % interval) is read on the walking goals. The goal count by the existing rule stands
  beside it; where the two differ the table gives both and the text says what the difference consists of.
  Velocity error stays over the trials that are goals by the existing rule, and is also given over the
  walking goals.
- *Unit of repetition.* The five robots of a run each start on their own copy of the same settled sand, so
  the 40 trials of a cell come from 8 settled beds. Beside every count strictly between 0 and the number of
  trials the goals per run are given, and where a verdict rests on such a count an exact test over the runs
  is given beside the test over trials.

**Added 2026-11-26, 04:19, before the change is tested: recording 2 s behind the goal line, and its test on one
baseline cell (asked by the lead; entry of 04:13).** The evaluation script gets an option that keeps a robot
walking after it has passed the goal line: the planar ramp and the sand records are made 3.0 m longer behind
the line (today the task ends an episode about 0.3 m behind the line, where the lane's workspace ends), and
for 2.0 s after the goal sample the script records whether a termination of the task fires for that robot,
which one, and when. The goal sample, the goal by the existing rule and the recorded duration of the trial stay
as they are; the option is off by default, so every earlier command gives the earlier run. Nothing in the task
code changes.

- *Per trial that is a goal, the hold outcome:* "held" (no termination in the 2.0 s), or the name of the
  termination that fired (pelvis too low, tilted past the limit, both: a fall; `terrain_out_of_bounds`: the
  robot reached the end of the lengthened lane, not a fall; `time_out`: the horizon ended first) with the time
  after the goal sample. From now on a **walking goal** of a cell run with the option is a walking goal by
  the entry of 04:13 whose hold outcome is "held".
- *Test cell.* Planar ramp +15° on the current scene (bundle made from the worktree now; its commit and the
  sha256 of the two walking scene files are recorded), MJWarp `3qymp37c` and RFT `6t0h2ud8`, beach sand, 0.50 m
  bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed setting, 40 trials per policy, the read-out of the shin-foot pair on, the option
  at 2.0 s and 3.0 m. Five robots per run and seeds 41 to 48 if the longer bed fits the GPU's memory; if not,
  four robots per run and seeds 41 to 50, and the record says so. A local run with three robots comes first
  (not a result).
- *What the test has to show for the change to count as proven.* (a) The first recorded pelvis position and
  joint angles of every trial equal those of the cell without the option on the same scene
  (`t3-asset-p15-*`; below 1e-5), if the number of robots per run is the same. (b) The goals by the existing
  rule do not differ from that cell's (Fisher's exact test at 0.01, per policy; difference with its 95 %
  interval). (c) Every goal trial has a hold outcome, and RFT's goals are "held" in at least 36 of 40: a
  walker has to stay a walker on the lengthened lane, otherwise the change itself disturbs the run and is
  not used.
- *The question the cell answers, reading fixed now.* Of MJWarp's goals by the existing rule: "MJWarp's
  line-crossers fall behind the line" if at least 90 % are terminated by a fall within the 2.0 s; "they do
  not fall behind the line" if at most 10 % are; otherwise the share is given as it is. Beside it: the time
  and the distance behind the line at which each falls, the count that reaches the end of the lane first, and
  the walking goals of both policies with and without the hold part. Known when this is written: without
  the stop on the old code 26 of 40 MJWarp trials pass the line at +15°, 4 of them are terminated within
  0.18 s after it and nothing is recorded beyond 0.3 m (fourteenth check); the cell without the option on the
  current scene is running and not read.

**Added 2026-11-26, 04:55, before the runs: two additions to the entry of 04:53 (asked by the lead).**

- *D4, the plain repeat.* The short-lane cell rerun unchanged (lane 9.37 m, nothing recorded behind the line,
  MJWarp only, seeds 41 to 48, five robots per run) on the bundle of D2 and D3. It answers whether 39 against
  26 is larger than what two submissions of one arrangement give. What is known of that scatter before the
  run: with the stop at planar +10°, two submissions from the same starts gave 9 and 7 of 40 with 4 robots
  reaching the goal in both; without the stop at planar +15°, the old code with the contact filtered and the
  current scene (different code that acts alike) gave 26 and 26 of 40, with 19 robots reaching the goal in
  both and 14 trials differing in outcome. So single trials of an edge cell flip between submissions, and
  the two counts available for the short lane are equal. *Reading:* 39 against 26 is "beyond the scatter
  between submissions" if D4 differs from the cell of 39 (Fisher's exact test at 0.01) and not from the cell
  of 26; "within the scatter" if D4 differs from the cell of 26 or does not differ from the cell of 39. In
  the second case neither the lane's end nor the tile grid is claimed, whatever D2 and D3 give.
- *The sand state itself.* If the place of the lane on the tile grid is the cause, it is a property of the
  streaming sand that T1 has to know. From the code: the patch that follows the robot is snapped to a 0.2 m
  grid anchored at the lane's origin, the tile records are indexed on a 0.2 m grid anchored at the world
  origin; the lane's origin lies 0.015 m off the world grid in the short-lane cell and 0.115 m off it in the
  long-lane cell. What would show the sand differing with that offset: (1) the number of live particles in
  each robot's patch over the run (a patch that loses or gains sand as it moves); (2) the sand top beside the
  robot over the run; (3) the particles in the column under a foot before it takes load. From the runs at
  hand: (2) is −8.6 cm in both cells from the fifth sample on; (3) early on the slope, 2.0 to 3.4 m from the
  start: RFT 1,732 against 1,726 particles, MJWarp 1,556 against 1,574, sand top under the sole and band
  counts within 1 %. Nothing that separates the cells. (1) was not recorded; D2, D3 and D4 record it per
  sample. *Reading:* "the sand differs with the place of the lane" if the live particle count per patch,
  from 2 s on, differs between two cells by more than the largest difference between the runs of one cell;
  otherwise "no difference of the sand shown by these measures".

**Added 2026-11-26, 05:27, before the runs: is it the tile grid or the MPM voxel grid? (asked by the lead after
D2 to D4)** The two arrangements differ by 0.100 m in the lane's origin. The lead's point: that is also a shift
against the MPM grid, so the phase against the voxels could matter with no tile involved.

- *Where things are anchored, from the code (read, not run).* The MPM grid is a sparse voxel grid on the world
  lattice (voxel index = floor(position / voxel size); `solver_implicit_mpm.py`, `allocate_by_voxels`). The
  settled sand template is placed tile by tile on the world tile grid (`particle_cache_kernels.py`: position =
  tile index × 0.2 m + position in the template, alternate tiles mirrored, each particle set on the surface
  under it), so the sand is in the same place against the voxel grid whatever the lane's origin is. The patch
  that follows the robot is stepped on a grid anchored at the lane's origin. The ramp, the start and the robot
  are placed in the lane's frame. So moving the lane's origin moves the ramp and the robot against the sand
  tiles and against the voxels, and moves the patch's steps against the tile records.
- *The voxel size.* These evaluation cells use a voxel of **0.02 m** (the script's default; the trainings use
  0.04 m). Offsets of the five cells at hand, lane origin against the world grids: cell of 26, D4 and D3:
  0.015 m against 0.2 m, 0.015 m against 0.04 m, 0.015 m against 0.02 m. D2 and the cell of 39: 0.115 m
  against 0.2 m, 0.035 m against 0.04 m, **0.015 m against 0.02 m**. So against the grid the evaluation
  solves on, the two arrangements already have the same phase (they are five voxels apart), and the phase
  against a 0.02 m grid cannot be what separates them; a period of 0.04 m (two voxels here, one voxel in the
  trainings) is not excluded by them.
- *Cells.* MJWarp only, planar ramp +15°, current scene, bundle `cadd2b3248822bf9`, no recording behind the
  line, seeds 41 to 48, five robots per run, 40 trials each, starts identical in the lane's frame. The lane's
  origin is moved by lengthening the lane before the start (`--behind`; a lengthening of 2 s moves the origin
  by s). Shifts from the arrangement of the cell of 26: **E1** 0.04 m (two voxels; 0.055 m against the tile
  grid); **E2** 0.02 m (one voxel; 0.035 m against the tile grid; half of a 0.04 m period); **E3** 0.20 m
  (one tile; must reproduce 26); **E4** 0.08 m (0.095 m against the tile grid, nearly half a tile; whole
  0.04 m periods); **E5** 0.06 m (0.075 m against the tile grid; half of a 0.04 m period); and one cell of
  mine, **E6** 0.01 m (half a voxel of the evaluation's 0.02 m grid; 0.025 m against the tile grid).
- *Reading, fixed now.* A cell is "like the cell of 26" if its goals differ from the cell of 39 and not from
  the cell of 26 (Fisher's exact test at 0.01), "like the cell of 39" in the mirror case, otherwise
  "between". **"A 0.04 m period"** (the lead's voxel phase) if E2 and E5 are like the cell of 39 and E1, E3
  and E4 like the cell of 26. **"The 0.02 m voxel phase"** if E6 is like the cell of 39 and E2 and E1 like
  the cell of 26. **"The tile phase"** if E3 is like the cell of 26, E4 like the cell of 39, and the count
  does not fall from E2 to E1 to E5 to E4 by more than 3 of 40 at any step (it moves with the offset against
  0.2 m, not with the offset against 0.04 m). Anything else: "not decided", with the six counts, the goals
  per run, and the pelvis height and speed at 4.4, 5.4 and 6.4 m.

**Amendment of 2026-11-26, 05:31, to the entry of 05:27, before any E cell is read (asked by the lead after
rss-T1 traced the code).** State of the cells when this is written: E3 and E2 were submitted at 05:29 and all
16 of their workflows are still pending on OSMO (none has started); no result or log of an E cell exists, and I
have seen none. E1, E5, E4 and E6 were not submitted; their queue is stopped.

- *What T1 traced, which I had not (`outputs/T1.md`, "Where the streaming grids are anchored").* With the tile
  cache on, the patch centre is first snapped on the grid anchored at the environment's origin and then
  overwritten by the centre snapped on the world grid, and that overwritten centre decides which particles are
  live. With a pool of 8 tiles the live window is therefore shifted against the robot's own grid by
  s = −r if r < 0.1 m and s = 0.2 m − r if r > 0.1 m, where r is the remainder of the lane's origin against
  0.2 m. The cells of 26 (r = 0.015 m) have s = −0.015 m; the cells of 36 and 39 (r = 0.115 m) have
  s = +0.085 m: about 10 cm more live sand on the +x side.
- *The two things T1 could not check, checked from my scene and results.* The pool has 8 tiles: the scene's
  streaming window is 1.2 m with a margin of 0.2 m on each side and 0.2 m tiles, the evaluation script does
  not change it, and every run starts with 346,112 live particles per robot = 64 tiles of 5,408. +x is uphill:
  the planar ramp rises along +x and the robot walks toward +x. So s > 0 means more live sand ahead of the
  robot, uphill.
- *What this does to the reading of 05:27.* All six cells E1 to E6 have r below 0.1 m (0.055, 0.035, 0.015,
  0.095, 0.075, 0.025), so s is between −0.015 and −0.095 m in all of them, and under T1's mechanism none
  should look like the cell of 39. My criterion for "the tile phase" asked E4 (r = 0.095) to be like the cell
  of 39; that was written without this trace and is the wrong prediction if T1's mechanism holds. The three
  outcomes of 05:27 stay as written; a fourth is added and three cells are added so that it can be told from
  them.
- *Added cells, as E1 to E6 in everything else.* **E7** origin shifted by 0.14 m (r = 0.155, s = +0.045 m).
  **E8** shifted by 0.18 m (r = 0.195, s = +0.005 m). **E9** shifted by 0.09 m (r = 0.105, just above 0.1:
  s = +0.095 m, the largest shift of the window that this lane can have).
- *Fourth outcome, fixed now: "the live window's shift (T1's mechanism)"* if (a) each of E1 to E6 is like the
  cell of 26 or lower, where "lower" means its goals are fewer than the cell of 26's and it differs from the
  cell of 39 by Fisher's exact test at 0.01 (a cell with more goals than the cell of 26 that does not differ
  from the cell of 39 breaks this); (b) E9 is like the cell of 39; (c) E8 is like the cell of 26; and (d) E7
  has at least as many goals as E8 and at most as many as E9, give or take 3 of 40. If (a) to (d) hold the
  count follows s and jumps across r = 0.1 m. If E4 is like the cell of 39 while E9 is too, the outcome is
  "the tile phase" of 05:27 and T1's sign rule does not describe it. Anything else: "not decided", with all
  counts against r and against s.
- *The shift is measured, not only inferred.* From now on each run records, at every sand sample (every
  0.5 s), the extent along x of each robot's live sand relative to its pelvis: nearest and farthest live
  particle behind and ahead, for all live particles and for those with mass (the fixed ring has none). The
  window's centre against the pelvis and the live sand ahead are read from that. E3 and E2 were submitted
  before this was added and do not have the record; E1, E4, E5, E6, E7, E8 and E9 are submitted on a bundle
  that has it. The reading above is on the goal counts; the measured shift is reported beside them, and if
  it does not follow T1's formula that is said first.

**Added 2026-11-26, 06:00: rule for cells at the edge of a policy's ability, for the evaluation of the
retrained policy (from Check 15, section E; asked by the lead).** Written before any checkpoint of `j0scsgdy`
is evaluated and before any E cell is read.

- *What every run records.* The origin of each robot's frame in the world, its remainder r against the
  0.2 m sand tile and the window shift s that follows from it (s = −r for r below 0.1 m, 0.2 m − r above),
  and the measured extent of the live sand against the pelvis (`sand_window_x`). `eval_walk.py` writes all of
  these from now on. Every table states the evaluation voxel (0.02 m; the trainings use 0.04 m), r and s.
- *Reference arrangement.* The present lanes stay as they are, so that new cells compare with the merged
  ones: r = 0 on the slope tile, r = 0.015 m on the planar ramp (live window centred on the robot within
  1.5 cm, as in training). A lane is lengthened only by multiples of 0.4 m (the lane is centred on the world,
  so the origin moves by half the lengthening and r is kept); with the recording behind the line that is
  `--hold_distance 3.2`.
- *Edge cells.* A cell on which a statement rests and whose goals or walking goals lie strictly between 0 and
  the number of trials is run at three window shifts with the same starts and seeds: the reference, and the
  lane's origin moved by 0.05 m and by 0.15 m against the reference (on the slope tile r = 0.05 and 0.15 m,
  s = −0.05 and +0.05 m). Goals, walking goals and goals per run are reported per shift. The three **agree**
  if the largest and the smallest of the three counts differ by at most 4 of 40, for goals and for walking
  goals (repeats of one arrangement differed by at most 1 of 40 so far: 26, 26, 25). If they agree the
  statement is made with the reference count and the range. If they do not, no single count is quoted: the
  range over the three shifts is, and a comparison between two policies is stated only if it holds, by its
  own test, at each of the three shifts. The origin is never drawn at random inside a cell.
- *Beside every count of such a cell:* the paired difference of the pelvis height on the level approach
  (pelvis between 0.3 and 1.5 m, trials paired by start) against the reference cell, with the number of
  trials of each sign.
- *Not part of this rule yet:* a control with a wider live window per edge cell (Check 15, section E, item
  4). Whether a wider window is affordable and what it shows is tested first on MJWarp at planar +15° (next
  entry, when it is written); the rule is extended or not after that, with the lead.
**Added 2026-11-26, 06:02, before the runs: a control with a wider live window (asked by the lead after
Check 15, item 7).** No E cell has been read when this is written.

  pinned ring comes within reach of MJWarp's trailing foot). No run varies the window alone. If the window is
  made wider, so that its ring is out of the feet's reach at both r, does the dependence on r go away?

  1.6 m in place of 1.2 m. The ring (0.2 m), the tile (0.2 m), the voxel (0.02 m), the awake box around the
  pelvis (1.2 m) and everything in the task and the solver stay as they are. The pool is then 2.0 m = 10
  tiles, an even number as before, so the shift formula is the same; the ring's inner edge lies 0.8 m from
  the window's centre in place of 0.6 m. The awake box's edge (0.6 m from the pelvis) stays, and is the same
  for every r. Read back from a short local run: 540,800 live particles per robot (100 tiles of 5,408), live
  extent 2.0 m, with mass 1.6 m.
- *Robots per run.* The wider window needs about 9.3 GB of GPU memory per robot (local runs with 1 and 2
  robots: 14.7 and 24.0 GB in all), so five robots do not fit on a 46 GB GPU. The control runs **4 robots per
  run and seeds 41 to 50: 40 trials**. Their starts are therefore not the starts of the merged cells, so two
  twins with the 1.2 m window and the same 4 robots and 10 seeds are run beside them. If a run with 4 robots
  fails for lack of memory, the four cells are run with 2 robots and seeds 41 to 60 instead.
- *Cells.* MJWarp only, planar ramp +15°, beach sand, 0.5 m bed, current scene, pool `isaac-lab-l40s-03` for
  all four (so the comparison is within one pool and GPU model), bundle named with the results. **V1**: window
  1.2 m, r = 0.015 m (`--behind 1.5`). **V2**: window 1.2 m, r = 0.115 m (`--behind 1.7`, lane 9.57 m).
  **W1**: window 1.6 m, r = 0.015 m. **W2**: window 1.6 m, r = 0.115 m.
- *Reading, fixed now.* First the twins: "the dependence is there in this design" if V2 has at least 8 of 40
  more goals than V1 or at least 8 of 40 more walking goals (with the 5-robot starts it was 10 to 14 goals
  and 10 to 16 walking goals). If it is not, the control is "not decided: the 4-robot design does not show
  the dependence", and the four counts are given. If it is: **"a wider window removes the dependence"** if W2
  and W1 differ by at most 4 of 40 in goals and by at most 4 of 40 in walking goals; **"the dependence
  remains with the wider window"** if W2 has at least 8 more goals or at least 8 more walking goals than W1;
  otherwise "not decided". Beside the counts: goals per run, the paired difference of the pelvis height on
  the level approach (pelvis between 0.3 and 1.5 m; W2 minus W1 and V2 minus V1, trials paired by start,
  number of each sign), and the measured window (`sand_window_x`).
- *What the level of W1 and W2 would say, if the dependence is removed (stated now so that it is not chosen
  afterwards).* Both with at most 4 walking goals: with the ring out of the feet's reach MJWarp does not walk
  +15°, as with the centred 1.2 m window; the shifted window overstated it. Both with at least 10 walking
  goals: the centred 1.2 m window understates MJWarp at +15°, and the merged statement "MJWarp does not walk
  +15°" must be requalified as a property of the 1.2 m window. Between: the counts are given without a
  sentence. In no case is a merged cell rescored; a change of the window for the evaluation of the retrained
  policy is Junnosuke's decision.
- *What this control cannot show.* Whether the awake box's edge matters (it is not moved); what the sand next
  to the ring does under a foot; anything about RFT or MPM, which are not run.

  the paired trials, against L88 the Mann-Whitney test on the two sets of 40, p at least 0.01 in either. It

- *Why.* T1 finds (unchecked) that in this evaluation's setting all live sand under a pressed box is asleep
  before contact and wakes in patches, with a press force that collapses and recovers, while the same press
  without the tile cache rises steadily. My own earlier cells have MJWarp at +15° on the slope tile with the
  stop at 0 of 40 with the task's sleeping and 23 of 40 with the sand kept awake. The window effect (0
  walking goals at 1.2 m, 25 at 1.6 m) could be that seen from another side.
- *Cells.* MJWarp only, planar ramp +15°, beach sand, 0.5 m bed, current scene, voxel 0.02 m, r = 0.015 m,
  default start and settling, 4 robots per run and seeds 41 to 50, the starts of V1 and W1; pool
  `isaac-lab-l40s-03`. Only the sleeping is changed against V1 and W1. **B12, B16: no sleeping at all**
  (`--sleep off`, the task's preset `solver_cfg_all_active_cache`: tile cache on, every live particle
  simulated, no awake box), live window 1.2 m and 1.6 m. **A12, A16: the sand in the box kept awake**
  (`--sleep region`, as in bed setting W: strain planner off, every particle inside the 1.2 m box around the
  pelvis awake, sand outside it asleep), live window 1.2 m and 1.6 m.
- *Reading, fixed now.* On walking goals of 40. **"The fixed wall, not sleeping"** if B16 has at least 8 more
  than B12: the window matters when no sand sleeps. **"Sleeping, not the wall"** if B12 and B16 are within 4:
  with no sleeping the window no longer matters. Otherwise "not decided". Separately, **"sleeping limits
  MJWarp at the 1.2 m window"** if B12 has at least 8 walking goals (V1, with the task's sleeping, has 0).
  Both sentences can hold together. For A12 and A16 the same thresholds give "with the sand in the box kept
  awake the window still matters" (A16 at least 8 above A12) or "does not" (within 4). Goals, goals per run,
  the paired pelvis height on the level approach against V1 and W1, and the measured window are given
  beside.
- *What the outcomes mean, stated now.* "The fixed wall": the size of the streaming window limits this
  policy whatever the sleeping, and the question goes to T1 as a property of the moving patch. "Sleeping":
  the task's sleeping setting is what makes the 1.2 m window fail for this policy; the cells of bed setting W
  (sand kept awake) are then the ones to read MJWarp's limit from, and T1's press test and my 0 against 23
  of 40 are the same effect. If B12 walks and B16 walks more, both act. In no case is a merged cell

**Amendment of 2026-11-26, 07:07, to the entry of 07:01, before any cell of it is read: one-robot twins for
L88.** Seen when written: nothing of the entries of 06:56, 06:57, 07:01 or 07:06 (two runs of L88 and two of
N24 are submitted as a memory test and have not been looked at).

- *Why.* A reading of the solver (T3-shin-notes, "What differs with the number of robots per run") shows that
  a run with one robot is a different solver configuration from a run with two or more (`separate_worlds` is
  switched off for a single world, and the convergence test is another one), and that with several robots
  the sand of one robot shares the iteration count and the warm-start resets with the others. L88 has to run
  with one robot for memory, so it cannot be compared with the 2- and 4-robot cells without a link.
- *Added cells.* **R12** and **R16**: MJWarp, planar +15°, r = 0.015 m, square live window 1.2 m and 1.6 m,
  1 robot per run and seeds 41 to 80, the starts of L88; pool `isaac-lab-l40s-03`.
- *Reading, added.* L88 is compared with R16 and R12 trial by trial (same starts): "the long-window level is
  reached at 1.6 m in the one-robot design" if R16's walking goals are within 4 of 40 of L88's and the paired
  pelvis height on the level approach does not differ (Wilcoxon, p at least 0.01). The comparison of L88
  with N16, N20, N24 and L40 (07:01) stays, and is read only if the one-robot and two-robot designs agree at
  the same window: R16 and N16 within 4 of 40 walking goals. If they do not, that is said first, with R12
  against V1 (0) and R16 against N16 and W1 (25), as the size of the effect of the number of robots.
**Added 2026-11-26, 07:40, after Check 16 and before the runs: robots per run against bundle; a 2-robot twin at
1.2 m; and a correction of what I wrote about the starts.** Seen when written: Check 16; the shift cells and

  in the run. Check 16 shows otherwise: 32 of the 40 starts of the 4-robot cells are starts of the 5-robot
  cells. A start depends on the seed and on the robot's place in the run, not on how many robots the run
  has. So cells with different numbers of robots share starts: 4 against 5 robots 32 (seeds 41 to 48, the
  first four robots), 2 against 4 robots 20 (seeds 41 to 50, the first two), 2 against 5 robots 16, 1 against
  2 robots 20 (seeds 41 to 60, the first robot), 1 against 4 robots 10, 1 against 5 robots 8.
- *What Check 16 found on those starts.* With 4 robots per run the pelvis rides 2.6 mm higher on the level
  approach than with 5 (V1 against E0, 25 of 32 higher, p = 0.001); walking goals 20 against 13 and 7 of 32
  at s = +0.085 m. My "no difference shown" is withdrawn. The two cells also differ in bundle.
- *Sizes of the window are compared only within one number of robots per run.* W1 against V1 (4 robots);
  N16, N20, N24 and L40 against a 2-robot twin at 1.2 m (Q2, below); R16 and L88 against R12 (1 robot).
- *Cells, all on one bundle (named with the results; it adds the record of the sand's sleep state) and on
  pool `isaac-lab-l40s-03`:* MJWarp, planar +15°, r = 0.015 m, live window 1.2 m, the task's sleeping.
  **Q5**: 5 robots per run, seeds 41 to 48. **Q4**: 4 robots, seeds 41 to 50. **Q2**: 2 robots, seeds 41 to
  60. (One robot per run: R12, already submitted on the bundle before this one, which differs from it only
  in the sleep record.)
- *Reading, fixed now; the paired measure first.* The measure is the pelvis height on the level approach
  (pelvis between 0.3 and 1.5 m), on the starts two cells share, Wilcoxon signed-rank test; two cells
  **differ** if p is below 0.01. **"The bundle changes the result"** if Q4 differs from V1 (40 shared starts,
  same pool, 4 robots, bundles `aea8587d` and the new one) or Q5 differs from E0 (40 shared starts, 5
  robots, bundles `13a05d3a` and the new one). **"The number of robots per run changes the result"** if Q4
  differs from Q5 on their 32 shared starts. If the first does not hold and the second does, Check 16's
  finding stands on one bundle; if neither holds, the difference of V1 against E0 is not reproduced and that
  is said. Q2 against Q4 (20 starts), Q2 against Q5 (16) and R12 against Q2 (20) are reported with the same
  test. Goals and walking goals on the shared starts are given second.
- *For the long window (amendment of the reading of 07:01).* L88 shares 20 starts with N16, N20, N24 and L40.
  The committed test against L88 (Mann-Whitney on the two sets of 40) stays the one named; the paired test

- *Why.* Over all cells the walking goals rise with the least distance of simulated sand ahead of the
  pelvis and are not ordered by the distance behind. No pair of cells has the same sand ahead and different
  sand behind, so whether the rear edge contributes is open.

  --behind 1.66`, s = −0.095 m): by the formula sand with mass at least 0.605 m ahead of the pelvis and
  0.795 m behind it. 4 robots per run, seeds 41 to 50, the starts of V2 (1.2 m window, s = +0.085 m: 0.58 m
  ahead, 0.42 m behind) and of W1 and W2. Pool `isaac-lab-l40s-03`, the bundle of the entry above.
- *Reading, fixed now; paired measures first.* Five measures, trials paired by start, Wilcoxon signed-rank,
  different if p is below 0.01: pelvis height on the level approach; pelvis height and forward speed at
  4.4 m and at 5.4 m (trials that reach the station in both cells). **"With the same sand ahead the rear
  edge changes nothing measurable"** if W95 and V2 differ in none of the five. **"The rear edge (or what
  else differs between the two windows) contributes"** if they differ in the level-approach measure or in
  at least two of the four station measures; the direction is given. Otherwise "not decided". Goals and
  walking goals are given second, with the share of the walking goals that sit within 0.1 of the speed
  bound.
- *What it cannot separate.* W95 also has a wider window across the lane and more live sand than V2; X16
  (1.6 m along, 1.2 m across) speaks to the width. The time between a strip of sand becoming live and a

  ahead" stay together here.

**Added 2026-11-26, 07:40: the rule for edge cells of 06:00, revised after Check 16, before any checkpoint of
`j0scsgdy` is evaluated.**

- *What was wrong with it.* It called three cells in agreement if their counts lay within 4 of 40 and said
  that repeats of one arrangement differed by at most 1 of 40. Repeats of one cell have since given 20, 20,
  25, 26 and 26 goals on the L40 pool and 31 on the L40S, and E9 gave 22 and 14 walking goals on the two
  pools. A threshold of 4 of 40 is below the scatter of repeats.
- *Revised.* Whether two cells with the same starts **agree** is tested first on paired continuous measures:
  the pelvis height on the level approach (pelvis between 0.3 and 1.5 m), and the pelvis height and forward
  speed at two stations on the slope (4.4 m and 5.4 m on the planar ramp; the same distances from the start
  on the slope tile), each over the trials that reach the station in both cells, Wilcoxon signed-rank test.
  Two cells agree if none of these differs at 0.01. Counts come second: goals and walking goals are given
  for each cell with the goals per run and compared by Fisher's exact test at 0.01. A difference in a count
  with no paired measure differing is reported as "the counts differ, the measures do not" and carries no
  statement. The thresholds "within 4 of 40" and "at least 8 of 40" stay in force only for the cells whose
  readings were committed with them (tonight's), where the paired measures are given beside and named
  first if they point another way.
- *Kept from 06:00.* The origin, r, s, the measured window, the window's size, the number of robots per run,
  the pool and the GPU are recorded and stated; lanes are changed only by multiples of 0.4 m; the origin is
  never drawn at random; within one comparison the number of robots per run, the pool and the bundle are
  the same.
- *Not fixed here.* Which arms and cells the evaluation of `j0scsgdy` has (window size, placement, sleeping,
  cache, voxel, bed, robots per run: Check 16, section C6) is Junnosuke's decision.
- *Two records asked for by the check.* The share of the sand with mass that is awake, fixed support and
  asleep, under the pelvis, 0.3 to 0.6 m ahead of it and over the whole window, is recorded from now on
  (`sand_sleep_share`, read from the sleeping planner every 0.5 s). The solver's iteration count is **not
  available**: it lives in a temporary of the solve and is not exposed; recording it needs a change in
  Newton, which is not mine to make.

**Amendment of 2026-11-26, 07:40, to the entry of 04:13 (walking goal), found by Check 16.** The entry says the
2.0 s window "ends with the sample in which the goal is scored". The code (`walking_goal.py`) takes the 100
samples before the trial's recorded end, so its window ends one sample (0.02 s) before that sample. Every
walking-goal count reported so far was computed with the code. **The definition is the code's: the window is
the 2.0 s of samples that end one sample before the goal sample.** The check finds three cells that change by
1 or 2 between the two versions. Also from the check, to be said beside the counts: at the shifted placement
the walking goals sit on the speed bound of 1.3 (median speed 1.27 to 1.37 of the command): the 10, 16 and 22
walking goals of D2, the cell of 39 and E9 would be 0, 3 and 5 with a bound of 1.2 and 29, 26 and 30 with
1.5. The zeros at the reference placement and the 0 against 25 between the 1.2 m and the 1.6 m window do not
depend on the bound.

**Added 2026-11-26, 14:25, before the runs: does the solver's iteration cap carry the effect of the number of
robots per run? (asked by the lead after Q2, Q4, Q5).** What I know when this is written: Q5, Q4, Q2 (MJWarp at
planar +15° with the task's sleeping and the 1.2 m window: 23, 30 and 40 goals and 0, 0 and 21 walking goals
with 5, 4 and 2 robots per run), and two short local runs of mine with 2 robots and no outcome counted: the
wall time of a control step of the simulation is 0.109 s with the task's cap of 25 iterations, 0.169 s with
100 and 0.451 s with 400, so the solve is cut at the cap and not ended by its tolerance of 1e-4; and the tile
cache's counter gives a pool change in 21.6 % of the solver steps with 2 robots walking.

- *The cause the code offers.* The stress and contact solve has one iteration count for all robots of a run
  and is cut at `max_iterations = 25`; every robot's contact warm start is cleared when any robot's pool
  changes. With more robots each solve starts cold more often and stops further from converged. If that is
  it, a higher cap should make a 5-robot run behave like a 2-robot run.
- *What is changed.* One script option, `eval_walk.py --max_iterations N`, which sets the sand solver's
  `max_iterations`; the tolerance (1e-4, now written into every result file) and everything else stay.
- *Cells.* Planar +15°, beach sand, 0.5 m bed, the task's sleeping, 1.2 m window, r = 0.015 m, the lane of Q5
  and Q2, pool `isaac-lab-l40s-03`, bundle `91f64fb03eb79e07`. MJWarp: **C5a** and **C5b**, 5 robots per run
  and seeds 41 to 48 (the starts of Q5), cap 100 and cap 400; **C2a** and **C2b**, 2 robots per run and seeds
  41 to 60 (the starts of Q2), cap 100 and cap 400 (C2b is mine, so that the 5-robot cell at 400 has a
  2-robot cell at the same cap). RFT, as a control that a walker stays a walker: **CR5a** and **CR5b**, 5
  robots, cap 100 and 400. If the runs at 400 prove unaffordable (more than about 45 minutes per run), the
  cap of the b cells is lowered to 200 and that is said.
- *Reading, fixed now.* Measures as before: the pelvis height on the level approach and the pelvis height
  and speed at 4.4 and 5.4 m, trials paired by start, Wilcoxon signed-rank at 0.01; two cells **differ** if
  the level-approach measure differs or at least two of the four station measures do. A 5-robot cell and a
  2-robot cell share 16 starts (seeds 41 to 48, the first two robots); a 5-robot cell and Q5 share all 40.
  For each raised cap: **"the iteration cap carries the effect of the number of robots"** if the 5-robot
  cell does not differ from the 2-robot cell at the same cap and differs from Q5; **"it does not"** if the
  5-robot cell does not differ from Q5; otherwise **"in part"**, with the numbers. Then, second, goals and
  walking goals of every cell. For RFT: "stays a walker" if it has at least 36 walking goals at the raised
  cap.
- *Given beside.* The 2-robot cells at the raised caps against Q2 (does the cap change a 2-robot run); for
  every cell the wall time of a control step and the share of solver steps with a pool change (new record
  `sand_change_count`, the tile cache's own counter, read after every control step).
- *What these cells cannot show.* Whether the cap acts through the cold starts or through the iteration
  count as such (the clearing is not switched off; that needs a change in the solver, which rss-T1 has
  proposed and nobody has applied); the solve's iteration count and residual per step (not exposed); 4
  robots per run; the cells without sleeping.
<!-- T3-axisA-definitions:end -->

<!-- T3-slope-tile:start -->
## The slope tile is a valley, not a planar slope (found 2026-11-25)

Status: found in this session while checking a longer tile; measured from the recorded surface heights of the
scored runs; reported to the lead; not yet checked by a fresh agent. A planar counterpart of the merged slope
runs is running (definitions block, entry of 2026-11-25, 19:20).

**What the tile is.** Every slope run of T3 so far uses the training sub-terrain as its slope:
`HfInvertedPyramidSlopedTerrainCfg` uphill and `HfPyramidSlopedTerrainCfg` downhill (Isaac Lab,
`isaaclab/terrains/height_field/hf_terrains.py`), one 16 m tile per robot, platform 2 m. The height of that tile
is the product of two ramps, `hf_raw = height_max * xx * yy`, with `xx` and `yy` falling linearly from 1 at the
tile centre to 0 at its edges. It is a planar slope only on the two axes through the centre. The walking test
starts the robot on the platform and sends it along the +X axis, which is a crease of the surface: uphill the
robot climbs the floor of a V-shaped valley, the ground rising to both sides; downhill it walks along a ridge,
the ground falling to both sides. Along the axis the slope is the nominal one; a foot standing beside the axis
is on ground that also slopes sideways by `slope * xx`, largest near the platform and falling to zero at the
tile edge.

**Measured in the scored runs** (surface heights recorded under the four corners of each sole; 16 m tile, MPM
policy, default bed setting):

| Nominal slope | Distance from the tile centre | Slope along the walk | Slope across, under a foot, rising outward | Product formula (sloped part 15.5 m wide, half-width 7.7 m) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| +30° | 3 m | 29.7° | 19 to 20° | 19.4° |
| +30° | 5 m | 29.2° | 11° | 11.4° |
| +30° | 6.5 m (the goal line is at 6.87 m) | 28.8° | 6° | 5.1° |
| +20° | 3 m | 19.4° | 12.6° | 12.5° |
| +20° | 5 m | 19.3° | 7.4° | 7.3° |
| +20° | 6.5 m | 19.3° | 3.6° | 3.3° |

So the angle along the walk is within about 1° of the nominal angle, and each foot stands on a side slope that
faces inward and flattens as the robot climbs. The slope along the walk is a little below nominal because the
robots walk 0.2 to 0.4 m beside the axis.

**What used this tile.** The slope sweep in every bed setting and bed depth (level ground is the same tile
with zero slope and is plane); the slope-by-friction grid; the sole, sand-flow and close-up analyses on slopes;
the whole-body comparison, the last seconds before RFT's falls, the steadiness measures and their trend
against slope; the swing-height and knee-below-the-surface numbers on slopes; the knee, hip pitch and ankle
torque-limit runs and the baselines with knee limits; the side-view clips at +20°, +25°, +30°. The analyses
of sand motion on slopes measure motion along the walking direction only; sand moving toward the valley floor
is not in them. Not affected: the material ladder and the density-friction grid (level ground), and the
towing runs of T2. The rigid-ground slope runs were made with a different test, `eval_rigid.py`, whose files do
not record the terrain; by its code it builds the same two pyramid tiles (found by the eleventh check), so the
rigid-ground slope results are on the valley tile too.

**Training.** The three policies trained on the same tile family: 8 m tiles, pyramid and inverted pyramid at 20 %
of the tiles each, slope 0 to 0.4 (21.8°) with the terrain level (MPM task
`velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/terrain_generator_cfg.py`; RFT and MJWarp tasks, baselines worktree,
`g1_29dof_soft_contact_mpm_align` and `g1_29dof_rigid_mpm_align`, `env_cfg/terrain_cfg.py`; present code, not
the runs' logged configurations). In training a robot starts on the platform with a random heading, so it
crosses the tile in any direction and meets the valley floor or the ridge only when it walks along an axis.
"Trained on slopes up to 21.8°" therefore means tiles whose slope along their axes is at most 21.8°; the
steepest ground on such a tile is at the platform's corner on the diagonal, 1.04 times the nominal slope
(22.5°; half-width of the sloped part 3.75 m). No policy was trained on a planar slope.

**What this changes.** The merged slope numbers stay as measured. Their description changes: they are results
on the training slope tile at a nominal angle, walked along its axis, not on a planar slope. Whether the valley
matters for the comparison of the policies is not known until the planar runs are in. One earlier planning row
of this file ("Proposed values for T3", sweep angles) says "planar ramp"; the runs did not use one, and the row
is corrected there. A larger tile is not a longer slope of the same kind: on a 56 m tile the side slopes are
27° at 3 m and 20° at 10 m, and in a local check 3 of 3 MPM robots fell there (local trials, not a result). A
standing start on a planar 30° ramp was also tried locally: 3 of 3 RFT robots fell within 2 s (1.5, 1.8, 1.5 s)
and 3 of 3 MPM robots walked for the whole 23 s; these are local trials, not a result. The planar runs
therefore start on a level strip, as the tile does.

**MJWarp at +10° against the hardware: what differs, as far as this session knows (the lead asked for the
list; nothing has been run on it).** In this simulation the MJWarp policy reaches 7 of 40 goals on a planar
+10° slope (4 of 10 on the tile) and none from +15°, while the task file says that on hardware "on flat ground
and a 10 degree slope every policy succeeded". What the simulated trial is: forward command 1.0 m/s with no
turning; 1.87 m of level sand, then 5 m of slope, goal line at its end, horizon 13 s; the robot is placed above
the sand and dropped onto it (default bed setting; the sand is settled for 2.5 s before, settled sand is put
to sleep by the task and wakes around the robot); beach sand as set in the task (density 1800 kg/m³, friction
0.80, no cohesion), 0.50 m deep over a rigid base; only the two feet touch sand (a 17 x 6 x 1 cm plate each),
shins and knees pass through it; checkpoint `3qymp37c` at iteration 19,999; no pushes, no mass, friction or
gain randomization in the test; a fall is the pelvis tilting past 45° or sinking to 0.2 m above the feet. Of
its 33 failures at +10°, 28 are the pelvis sinking too low and 5 the pelvis tilting, half of them within 5.3 s.
What is known to depend on the test in this simulation: the bed setting. At +15° MJWarp reaches 0 of 40 with
the task's sleeping (three settings) and 23 of 40 with the sand settled for 12 s and kept awake; at +10° only
the default setting was run (planar 7 of 40, tile 4 of 10), so the setting closest to a settled real bed has
not been run at 10°. What this session does not know about the hardware trial: the commanded speed, the
length of the slope and how success was counted, the number of trials, the sand (kind, depth, how it was
prepared, dry or damp), which checkpoint of which training run was on the robot, and the robot's actuator
limits. Any of these could carry the difference; none was checked.
<!-- T3-slope-tile:end -->

## P1-1 terrain geometry on a planar slope: counterpart of the slope sweep (2026-11-26)

Status: checked on 2026-11-25, eleventh check: every planar number reproduced from the raw files; corrections applied. Data: `logs/t3/final_1/planar/report/planar_compare.json`; scripts `scripts/benchmarks/t3/planar_compare.py`, `planar_slides.py`, terrain `ramp_terrain.py`.

<b>The same slope runs on a planar slope (checked on 2026-11-25, eleventh check: every planar number reproduced from the raw files; corrections applied; reading committed before the runs, task record, definitions block, entry of 2026-11-25, 19:20).</b> The slope tile of every other slope result on these slides is the training tile, a valley along the walk (conditions line above). Here the three policies walk a planar ramp: a level strip, then a plane rising along the walk with no slope across; the slope starts 1.87 m ahead of the start, the goal line is 5 m further, the horizon is 13 s and a robot more than 1.5 m to the side has left its lane, as on the tile (the planar mesh is 4 m wide, the tile 16 m). Beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed setting, 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 48, five robots per run, each on its own lane and sand). Goals of 40 on the planar slope, with the slope tile's count in brackets: +20°: MJWarp 0 / 40 (0), RFT 40 / 40 (40), MPM 40 / 40 (40); +25°: MJWarp 0 / 40 (0), RFT 40 / 40 (39), MPM 40 / 40 (40); +30°: MJWarp 0 / 40 (0), RFT 18 / 40 (13), MPM 40 / 40 (40). <b>By the rule: the slope result holds on a planar slope.</b> No terrain effect is shown for any policy and slope by the rule (Fisher's exact test at 0.01). "No change shown" is not "no effect": the largest difference between RFT's or MPM's counts is RFT at +30°, 18 against 13 of 40, +12 points with a 95 % interval of -9 to +32 points, so an effect of the tile of that size is not excluded; it cannot remove the gap to MPM (40 of 40). MJWarp, run alone on the shallower planar slopes: 7 / 40 at +10° (slope tile 4 / 10), 0 / 40 at +15° (slope tile 0 / 40); it reaches at most 3 of 40 goals from +15° on, as on the slope tile, so the slope at which it stops climbing does not move. These counts are with the stop; without it 40 of 40 at +10° and 26 of 40 at +15°, none of the 26 a walk (window of simulated sand centred on the robot, as in training; with the window 8.5 cm further uphill 36 and 39 of 40 pass the line and about a third walk, cause unchecked). The line above gives RFT 16 of 40 with the stop at +30°: that is the control arm of the runs with and without the shin-foot contact (same starts, a later run); the run of this table has 18 of 40. Every count in this table is with the shin-foot stop. No policy was trained on a planar slope; the training terrain is the slope tile.

## The slope and level runs with and without the shin-foot contact (2026-11-26)

Status: checked on 2026-11-25 and 2026-11-26, twelfth and thirteenth check: every number reproduced from the raw files; the planar +25°, +15° and +10° cells checked on 2026-11-26, fourteenth check: every number reproduced from the raw files. Data: `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/report/shin_contact.json`; scripts `scripts/benchmarks/t3/shin_contact_compare.py`, `shin_slides.py`, option `eval_walk.py --no_shin_foot_contact` (`shin_foot_filter.py`).

<b>The same runs with and without the shin-foot contact (checked on 2026-11-25 and 2026-11-26, twelfth and thirteenth check: every number reproduced from the raw files; the planar +25°, +15° and +10° cells checked on 2026-11-26, fourteenth check: every number reproduced from the raw files; reading committed before the runs, task record, definitions block, entry of 2026-11-25, 20:45).</b> In the MPM tasks the feet's collision shapes carry a contact margin of 2 cm, which the sand needs. The contact between the robot's own links reads the same margin, so the shin meets the foot 2 cm early and the ankle pitch joint stops at about −0.46 rad, 26 of its 50 degrees toes-up. This holds for every run on MPM sand on these slides, and for the training of the MPM policy; the RFT and the rigid training have no such stop. Here every cell is run twice in one workflow on the same GPU type: as it is, and with that one contact removed in the evaluation (the shin and foot shapes of each leg are added to the collision filter; margin, sand contact and every other contact unchanged; no policy retrained). Beach sand unless named, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s; slopes in the default bed setting with 40 trials per cell and arm, level ground in setting T (start on the settled surface) with 10. Goals with the contact → without it: slope tile, +30°: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 13 → 28, MPM 40 → 1; planar slope, +30°: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 16 → 39, MPM 40 → 0; slope tile, +25°: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 40 → 40, MPM 40 → 36; planar slope, +25°: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 40 → 40, MPM 40 → 40; slope tile, +20°: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 40 → 40, MPM 40 → 40; planar slope, +20°: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 40 → 40, MPM 40 → 40; planar slope, +15°: MJWarp 0 → 26, RFT 40 → 40, MPM 40 → 40; planar slope, +10°: MJWarp 9 → 40, RFT 40 → 40, MPM 40 → 40; level ground, beach sand: MJWarp 10 → 10, RFT 10 → 10, MPM 10 → 10; level ground, loose poppy seed: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 10 → 10, MPM 10 → 10. <b>By the rule: The separation at +30° was the shin-foot contact.</b> By the rule the contact changes the outcome for RFT on the slope tile, +30° (13 to 28 of 40); MPM on the slope tile, +30° (40 to 1 of 40); RFT on the planar slope, +30° (16 to 39 of 40); MPM on the planar slope, +30° (40 to 0 of 40); MJWarp on the planar slope, +15° (0 to 26 of 40); MJWarp on the planar slope, +10° (9 to 40 of 40). At +25° on the tile the MPM checkpoint's 36 goals of 40 without the contact are lower and 12 to 23 % faster than commanded (pelvis 0.41 against 0.45 m, every one below the lowest trial with the stop); on level loose poppy seed its 10 of 10 are wading, 9 % faster than commanded, with the torso 10° forward. On the planar ramp without the contact MJWarp's 40 goals at +10° are a walk (pelvis 0.52 m, 1.01 m/s); its 26 goals at +15° are not: every one is lower and faster than any RFT trial (pelvis 0.39 against 0.55 m, 1.55 against 0.91 m/s at the end), and the 14 others fall at the time these cross the line. At the line the pelvis is 0.24 m above the ground and moves at 1.95 m/s with the torso 31° forward; with the line at 4 m of slope 38 would pass, at 4.5 m 35. <b>At +25°, by the rule: Without the stop the MPM policy still climbs +25°.</b> On the planar ramp in a steady gait 5 cm lower and 8 to 10 % faster than commanded; on the tile 5 of the 36 goals are crossings above 1.3 m/s. The MPM policy was trained with this contact and the two baselines without it, so for this one contact the runs without it are the fairer test of the baselines and an unfair one of the MPM policy (the foot plate thickened by 2 cm in the sand and the foot's margin against other rigid shapes remain in both arms). This is no comparison of training on particles with training on RFT: that needs an MPM policy trained without the contact, which is being trained.

<b>The same cells on the fixed code and on the scene without the robot's collision entry (checked on 2026-11-26, thirteenth check: every number reproduced from the raw files; readings committed before the runs, for the fixed code at +30° before its results: task record, entries of 2026-11-25, 22:26, 23:11 and 23:23, and of 2026-11-26, 00:40).</b> Two changes of the code followed the finding. First the feet's 2 cm contact margin was given to the sand solver only (rigid margin 0, contact gap 0: "fixed code"). Then the robot's own collision entry was taken out of the walking scene, so the robot is the asset as it is (rigid margin 0, contact gap 0.01 m, sand margin 0.02 m: "scene without the collision entry"). The MPM policy is being retrained on the fixed code (contact gap 0; W&B j0scsgdy, submitted before the entry was removed); later trainings start from the scene without the entry. The same three policies, seeds and starts, no evaluation option. The margins are read back from the built model in every run; the contact gap is read back on the scene without the entry only (the fixed-code runs did not record it; it is 0 by that bundle's scene files). The fixed code was run on the first four cells of the table (not on the planar ramp, +25° or +10°); the scene without the entry also on the planar ramp at +10° and +15° (those two rows unchecked). The MPM policy in these tables is the one trained with the stop. Slope tile: default bed setting, 40 trials. Level ground: setting T (start on the settled surface), 10 trials. <b>By the rules, in each of these cells and for every policy the fixed code acts like the arm without the contact, and the scene without the collision entry acts like the fixed code</b> (velocity errors within 0.005 m/s). Against the old setup with the stop the goals change at +30° only (RFT 13 to 29 of 40, the MPM checkpoint 40 to 0). At +20° and on level ground no policy's goal count changes. On level sand the stop had raised the baselines' velocity error: without it RFT's is 0.055 against 0.094 m/s on beach sand and 0.079 against 0.125 m/s on loose poppy seed, MJWarp's 0.045 against 0.088 m/s on beach sand (differences −0.039, −0.046 and −0.044 m/s, 95 % intervals −0.043 to −0.036, −0.051 to −0.041, −0.068 to −0.019; 37 to 49 % lower), because the forward speed inside a trial is twice as steady. The MPM checkpoint's rises from 0.075 to 0.100 m/s on loose poppy seed. These differences are below the 0.05 m/s threshold fixed beforehand, so the rule's label is "no change shown"; they are present in every trial, on the fixed code, on the scene without the entry and in both bed settings. Shin against foot on the scene without the collision entry, read in all 108 runs: contact candidates are listed in 36,588 of 36,687 control steps (the 0.01 m gap) and carry no force in any step of any run (0 runs with a force); the read-out sees forces on other rigid contacts in the same runs. <b>Forward lean (rule of 23:11):</b> without the stop the MPM checkpoint's torso moves forward on both sands: on loose poppy seed from 1.2° to 9.7° forward, on beach sand from 2.8° to 1.1° behind the vertical (it does not lean forward there); differences +8.5° and +1.7°, each many times the spread of the runs with the stop. The lean is in the waist; the pelvis does not pitch forward. The baselines' torsos move 3° to 5° back. This is consistent with the hardware hypothesis on loose poppy seed only; there is no forward lean on beach sand; the hardware lean is not recorded. On loose poppy seed without the stop the MPM checkpoint wades: both feet carry load in every sample and it walks 9 % faster than commanded.
