### Notes to the shin-foot runs (hand-written, 2026-11-25, unchecked)

- *Status of the cells (23:30).* In, on code bundle `300131cc8bc652ef`, each workflow with a control task and a
  filtered task on NVIDIA L40: slope tile +30° (`t3-shin-t30-s41…48-1`) and planar ramp +30°
  (`t3-shin-p30-s41…48-1`), both checked (twelfth check); slope tile +20° (`t3-shin-t20-s41…48-1`) and planar
  ramp +20° (`t3-shin-p20-s41…48-1`), unchecked. Queued on the same bundle: level beach sand and loose poppy
  seed, slope tile and planar ramp +25°, planar ramp +10°. On the bundle with the margin fix
  (`4517b02375887014`, no option): slope tile +30° running (`t3-agree-t30-s41…48-1`), level sands and slope
  tile +20° queued.
- *+20° (unchecked).* No policy's outcome changes on either terrain: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 40 → 40, MPM 40 → 40 of
  40 on the tile and on the planar ramp (each 0 points, 95 % interval −9 to +9). The filter acts (ankle 1st
  percentile −0.44 to −0.46 rad with, −0.85 to −0.90 rad without the contact). Velocity error of the goal
  trials, with → without: RFT 0.133 → 0.141 and 0.133 → 0.136 m/s, MPM 0.069 → 0.055 and 0.071 → 0.051 m/s
  (tile, planar). The goal trials end alike in both arms (pelvis 0.45 to 0.53 m above the ground, 0.86 to
  0.98 m/s). Sand under the sole per stance, with → without: RFT 3.5 → 3.5 and 3.8 → 3.8 cm, MPM 2.2 → 4.8 and
  2.4 → 5.0 cm: with the stop less sand moves under the MPM policy's sole than under RFT's, without it more.
  One result file of the tile wave was missing after the first download and was fetched again (40 trials in
  every arm). The duplicate `t3-shin-p20-s48-2` agrees with its twin (goals 0, 5, 5 of 5 in both arms).
- *Does the option act, and only as meant?* Every filtered run records 10 filtered pairs of colliding shapes on
  5 robots (2 per robot: one shin shape against one foot shape per leg); the run stops if the count differs.
  With the contact the 1st percentile of the ankle pitch is −0.44, −0.45, −0.46 rad (MJWarp, RFT, MPM); without it
  −0.86, −0.89, −0.91 rad. The two arms of a seed start from identical states (first recorded pelvis position and
  joint angles equal for all 120 trials) and part within 0.02 to 0.08 s: the contact is active from the first
  control step of every run on MPM sand. Local check before the runs (three robots, RFT, planar ramp +30°): 6
  pairs on 3 robots, ankle pitch down to −0.905 rad; those three trials are not a result. A run that renders
  video also carries the links' visual shapes (8 pairs per robot, of which 2 collide); the check counts the
  colliding pairs.
- *What the tile +30° cell says, by the reading fixed before the runs.* The shin-foot contact changes the
  outcome of RFT (13 → 28 of 40, p = 0.0016, +37 points, 95 % interval +16 to +55) and of MPM (40 → 1 of 40,
  p = 8e-22, −98 points, interval −100 to −84); for MJWarp "not the contact" (0 and 0 of 40). Without the contact
  39 of the 40 MPM trials fall forward, at a median of 3.8 s and a median farthest pelvis position of 3.5 m
  (the slope starts at 1.87 m); RFT's 12 failures are falls forward. Without the contact RFT is better than MPM
  by the rule (28 against 1, p = 9e-11).
- *What the planar +30° cell says, and the rule for the separation (added when the cell came in, 22:36).* On
  the planar ramp the contact changes the outcome of RFT (16 → 39 of 40, p = 1e-08, +57 points, 95 % interval
  +39 to +71) and of MPM (40 → 0 of 40, p = 2e-23, −100 points, interval −100 to −88); MJWarp "not the contact"
  (0 and 0). Without the contact all 40 MPM trials fall forward, between 3.4 and 3.8 s, at a median farthest
  pelvis position of 3.3 m, that is a median of 1.5 m up the slope (1.3 to 1.7 m; the slope starts at 1.87 m;
  corrected by the twelfth check from "about 1.4 m"); RFT's one failure is a fall forward. In the arm without the contact RFT is better
  than MPM on both terrains (tile 28 against 1, p = 9e-11; planar 39 against 0, p = 8e-22), and the contact
  changes the MPM policy's outcome on both. **By the rule fixed before the runs: "The separation at +30° was
  the shin-foot contact."** The filter acts for all three policies on the planar ramp as well (ankle 1st
  percentile −0.44, −0.45, −0.46 rad with the contact; −0.84, −0.89, −0.89 rad without it; 10 pairs on 5 robots
  in every filtered run; the arms start from identical states). The planar control arm against the merged
  planar runs (bundle `1eb8610adc89fadf`): MJWarp 0 against 0, RFT 16 against 18 (−5 points, 95 % interval −25
  to +16, p = 0.82), MPM 40 against 40, starts equal: "no change shown".
- *What it does not say.* The MPM policy was trained with this contact, the baselines without it. The filtered
  arm is the fairer test of the baselines and an unfair one of the MPM policy: it shows that this policy as
  trained needs the contact at +30° on the tile, not that training on particles gives no advantage there. That
  question needs an MPM policy trained without the contact; a retraining is the user's decision and is not
  started. Every earlier statement on these pages about the MPM policy on slopes, and every statement about RFT
  and MJWarp on MPM sand, was measured with the contact; none of them is changed here until the planar cell is
  in and a check has run.
- *Is the change of code bundle neutral? (reading of 20:42, read on the control arm of this cell as agreed with
  the lead, in place of the separate rerun).* The control arm ran on bundle `300131cc8bc652ef`, which holds the
  changes of `1eb8610adc89fadf` and the new option, off in this arm; the merged runs on `bd2f5052f974fca8`.
  Goals of 40: MJWarp 0, RFT 13, MPM 40 against the merged 0, 13, 40. RFT: 0 points, 95 % interval −20 to +20,
  Fisher p = 1: "no change shown". The first recorded pelvis position and joint angles of all 120 trials equal
  those of the merged run once the seeds are matched (the merged file holds the seeds in the order 43 to 48,
  41, 42). The bundle named in the reading, `1eb8610adc89fadf`, was not rerun by itself.
- *Long climb (reading of 18:01, amendment of 19:20), first wave only, on hold.* Planar ramp +30°, default bed
  setting, 25 m of slope, 23 s, 40 trials (seeds 41 to 50, four robots per run), with the contact: all 40 RFT
  trials fall, the last at 14.3 s and 11.6 m up the slope (22 of 40 pass the 5 m line of the short runs, which
  count 18 of 40); 38 of 40 MPM trials walk at 23 s, and no measure drifts by the rule. The MPM half of this
  run rests on the contact as well. The other waves (+25°, +20°, setting W) are not submitted: whether they run
  with the contact, without it or both is the user's decision. Data `logs/t3/final_1/planar/long_default`,
  report `logs/t3/final_1/planar/long_report`; not on the slides.
- *Videos, so that the outcomes can be seen (one robot per run, slope tile +30°, side view, sand drawn as
  particles, without the shin-foot contact; rendered locally with the same option, 2 pairs of colliding shapes
  filtered per robot). I looked at the frames of both MPM clips.* On the page since version 80, in the collapsed
  video grid, labelled "without the shin-foot contact (unchecked)"; files `site/T3/media/gait_slope30_noshin_*`
  (listed in the generated video list of this record).
  - Start of seed 41: the MPM policy walks the level strip, sinks into a deep crouch on the first metres of the
    slope and goes down forward onto its knees at 4.0 s; RFT reaches the goal at 8.7 s; MJWarp falls at 3.4 s.
  - Start of seed 45 (the start of the page's +30° clips with the contact, so each policy has a same-start pair
    with and without the contact): MJWarp falls at 3.1 s (3.2 s with the contact); RFT reaches the goal at 8.8 s
    (8.0 s); the MPM policy crosses the goal line at 6.0 s (7.9 s with the contact), low and fast: over its
    last 2 s the pelvis is 0.29 m above the ground under it (0.39 m with the contact) and moves at 1.4 m/s
    against the commanded 1.0 m/s, leaning far forward on deep-bent knees.
  - How the clips relate to the counts: two single-robot runs of the MPM policy without the contact give one
    fall and one goal; the scored runs give 1 goal of 40 (Fisher's exact test of 1 of 2 against 1 of 40:
    p = 0.09, within chance). The one scored goal is of the same kind as the seed-45 clip: goal line at 5.8 s,
    pelvis 0.26 m above the ground over the last 2 s. The seed-45 clip therefore shows the rare outcome and the
    seed-41 clip the usual one; the captions say so. "Reaches the goal" is the scoring rule (pelvis past the
    goal line without a termination); for these two MPM trials it is not the gait of the runs with the contact.
  - Earlier copies of the seed-41 clips under `site/T3/media/shin30_without_contact_*_seed41.mp4` are the same
    recordings without the text label; they are not used by the page.
- *Checked (twelfth check, `checks/T3.md`, pass; merged into `FINAL.md`).* Every number of the two +30° cells
  reproduces from the raw trial files. Corrections applied here: the distance above (1.5 m); and the window of
  the ankle numbers. The 1st percentiles in the table and in these notes are taken over the samples from 1.5 s
  after the start to the end of each trial, with the last 0.5 s before a fall left out. Over all samples from
  0.5 s to the end the checker finds, with / without the contact: MJWarp −0.48 / −0.90, RFT −0.45 / −0.89, MPM
  −0.46 / −0.91 rad on the tile and −0.48 / −0.90, −0.45 / −0.89, −0.46 / −0.90 rad on the planar ramp: only
  MJWarp's values depend on the window (it falls at about 3.5 s, so little of its trial lies inside mine);
  every policy passes −0.50 rad either way. Without the contact the ankle goes past its joint limit of
  −0.873 rad (the limit is soft in the simulation) in 6 % of the samples for MJWarp and MPM and 17 to 18 % for
  RFT, to −0.91 to −0.97 rad (the checker's numbers). "The fairer test of the baselines" holds for the
  shin-foot pair only: the foot plate thickened by 2 cm in the sand and the 2 cm margin against other rigid
  shapes remain in both arms.
- *The filter file as run (issue Q3 of the twelfth check).* `shin_foot_filter.py` is untracked and was edited
  at 21:20, after the bundle was made (20:50). Read back from the bundle itself (`stack.tar.gz` of bundle
  `300131cc8bc652ef`, sha256 `300131cc8bc652ef46354d221370582ee27097d30fceb132dc3bc43994bd2911`): the file as
  run has sha256 `86a657175af109d2a968a32682c6a6db3ac3213f777b4c91e56a2bf9bf7fb794`; a copy is kept at
  `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/as_run/shin_foot_filter_bundle_300131cc8bc652ef.py`. The file in the worktree
  now has sha256 `53c1b98586e8af37ddd3096c22c83399ce5294cf5658b6510a400f7a8b89bd02`. The 21:20 edit changed the
  bookkeeping only: which pairs are added to the collision filter is the same code (every shape of
  `<side>_knee_link` against every shape of `<side>_ankle_roll_link` of the same robot); new are the count of
  the pairs in which both shapes collide (`pairs`, the number the run checks against 2 per robot), the count
  of all pairs (`all_pairs`), and the printed line ("pairs of colliding shapes" where the as-run file prints
  "shape pairs"). Reason: a run that renders video carries the links' visual shapes (8 pairs per robot, 2 of
  which collide), and the as-run check stopped it. In the scored, headless runs both counts are 10 on 5 robots.
  `eval_walk.py` in the bundle and in the worktree at the time of the check had the same sha256 (`22a19b0e…`,
  the one in every protocol); from now on `eval_walk.py` also records the filter file's sha256 in the protocol
  (`shin_foot_contact.filter_sha256`), which changes its own hash for later bundles.

  *Goals.* "No change shown" for every policy on both sands (with 10 trials only a gross change could show);
  MJWarp on loose poppy seed "not the contact" (0 of 10, falls forward, either way). *Velocity error.* By the
  thresholds of 02:54 (0.05 m/s and two pooled standard deviations) "no change shown" everywhere; the
  differences are nevertheless many standard deviations: without the stop RFT's error is 0.04 to 0.05 m/s
  lower on both sands and MJWarp's 0.04 m/s lower on beach sand, the MPM policy's 0.025 m/s higher on loose
  poppy seed. *Fixed code.* "Acts like the arm without the contact" for every policy on both sands (goals,
  velocity error within 0.001 m/s, ankle, lean; starts equal; margins 0 and 0.02 m read back). So on level
  ground the user's expectation holds for success (MJWarp and RFT reach the same goals on the fixed code); the
  baselines track the command slightly better there than on the old setup.
  *Forward lean of the MPM policy (reading of 23:11).* Beach sand: +1.7° (95 % interval +1.5 to +2.0; spread
  of the control arm 0.5°). Loose poppy seed: +8.5° (+8.3 to +8.6; spread 0.2°). **By the rule, on both sands:
  "the forward lean appears only without the stop."** What the label covers: on beach sand the torso goes from
  2.8° behind the vertical to 1.1° behind it, so it moves forward by 1.7° and still does not lean forward; on
  loose poppy seed it goes from 1.2° to 9.7° forward, a lean that can be seen, with the pelvis 2 cm lower and
  the robot 6 % faster than commanded. The pelvis itself does not pitch forward (beach sand 2.1° → −0.6°,
  loose poppy seed 3.1° → 3.1°): the lean is in the waist. RFT and MJWarp go the other way: their torso leans
  3° to 5° less without the stop. The fixed code gives the same lean as the arm without the contact (−1.0° and
  9.7°). This shows that removing the stop makes the simulated MPM checkpoint carry its torso further
  forward, most on the softest sand; it does not show that this is why the real robot leaned (not measured
  here). The stance table of the MPM policy on loose poppy seed without the contact has no trial with three
  detected stances, so its sole angle and sand motion there are not available.
  *Default bed setting, beach sand (second setting):* the same counts (10 / 10 / 10 for every policy), the
  same velocity errors to 0.006 m/s and the same lean (MPM −2.8° → −1.1°, +1.6°, spread 0.25°: same verdict);
  the two settings agree on level beach sand.

- *Checked (thirteenth check, `checks/T3.md`, pass with corrections; merged into `FINAL.md`; 2026-11-26).* All
  2,040 trials of the +20°, tile +25° and level cells, the fixed code, the scene without the collision entry,
  the foot numbers and the lean reproduce from the raw files. The corrections below hold for every note above
  that says otherwise; the generated block and the page (version 90) carry them.
  1. *Which code the MPM policy is retrained on.* `j0scsgdy` (and the cached run) train on the **fixed code
     with the feet's contact gap 0**; they were submitted before the collision entry was removed. My note and
     the page said "retrained on that scene" (the scene without the entry): wrong. Later trainings start from
     the scene without the entry. The two codes agree in every cell run on both.
  2. *"In every cell"* means the cells run on the fixed code: slope tile +30° and +20°, level beach sand in
     setting T and in the default setting, level loose poppy seed in setting T; on the scene without the entry
     the first two and the two level sands in setting T. Not run on either: the planar ramp, +25°, +10°, +15°,
     downhill, other sands, the sand kept awake.
  3. *Numbers.* The scene without the entry is within 0.005 m/s of the fixed code in velocity error (0.0045 at
     the most; I wrote 0.004 from rounded values). The fixed-code runs did not record the feet's contact gap
     (the read-out was added later); it is 0 by that bundle's two scene files. Bundle `b87ab0b19b0ad2ce` is
     Isaac Lab `819ff3622` with Newton `5ececcbe`; bundle `4517b02375887014` is Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with
     Newton `48231a98`; they differ in more files than the scene entry (collider option of the MPM solver, tow
     and soft-contact files), none of which acts in the walking task by the checker's reading.
  4. *Velocity error on level sand.* "No change shown" is the rule's label and hides a real change. Without
     the stop RFT's error is 0.055 against 0.094 m/s on beach sand and 0.079 against 0.125 m/s on loose poppy
     seed, MJWarp's 0.045 against 0.088 m/s on beach sand (differences −0.039, −0.046 and −0.044 m/s; 95 %
     intervals −0.043 to −0.036, −0.051 to −0.041, −0.068 to −0.019; 37 to 49 % lower), because the forward
     speed inside a trial is twice as steady. The MPM checkpoint's rises from 0.075 to 0.100 m/s on loose poppy
     seed. The differences are below the 0.05 m/s threshold fixed beforehand; they are present in every trial,
     on the fixed code, on the scene without the entry and in both bed settings. So "it should not change
     performance at all" holds for the goals of MJWarp and RFT at +20° and on level ground, not at +30°, and
     not for tracking on level sand, where both baselines track better without the stop. Consequence: the
     earlier statements that RFT tracks worse than MPM on sand were the stop on the two sands tested.
  5. *The MPM checkpoint's sole at +20°.* "8° → 15° below level" is the median over the stance from dig-in to
     two samples before lift-off, which includes the push-off at low load. Over the samples above 20 % of body
     weight it is 10° → 7°, above body weight 10° → 2° (the checker's values): with the stop the sole keeps one
     angle, without it it turns during the stance, level under the largest load and 20° below level before
     lift-off. The verdict "changes" holds in every measure; "goes the other way" needs this definition beside
     it. RFT and MJWarp move the same way in both measures.
  6. *The forward lean.* To be said as: without the stop the MPM checkpoint's torso moves forward on both
     sands, on loose poppy seed from 1.2° to 9.7° forward, on beach sand from 2.8° to 1.1° behind the vertical;
     consistent with the hardware hypothesis on loose poppy seed only; no forward lean on beach sand; the
     hardware lean is not recorded in this project (no angle, ground or checkpoint), so nothing can be compared
     with it. On loose poppy seed without the stop the checkpoint wades (both feet loaded in every sample, 9 %
     faster than commanded, torso 10° forward); MJWarp's 29° lean there with the stop comes from 2 of 10 trials.
  7. *Bed setting.* The first paragraph of the generated block gave "default bed setting" for every cell; the
     level rows are setting T (amendment of 23:59). Corrected in the generated text.
  8. *Order of one commit.* The note of 23:16 and the entry of 23:23 were committed 3.5 minutes after the
     fixed-code +30° cell was submitted and before any of its results existed; for that cell they are "before
     the results", not "before the run".
  9. *+10° and +15° have never been run without the stop,* on either terrain; this is where MJWarp's failure
     begins and where the hardware disagrees (all three policies climbed 10° on hardware). The planar +10°
     cell with and without the contact was submitted at 02:21 (`t3-shin-p10-s41…48-1`) and is running. A +15°
     cell with and without the contact (40 trials) follows it, with its reading written and committed first.
  10. *What stands without the stop (the check's summary).* Supported in simulation: the policy trained on
     rigid ground fails on beach-sand slopes from +20° and on level loose poppy seed, where the policy trained
     on RFT sand walks. Not supported yet: anything about what training on particles adds over RFT; the old
     MPM checkpoint without the stop is outside its training condition, and its one remaining advantage is
     tracking at +20°.

  720 trials of planar +10°, +15° and +25° with and without the stop reproduce. The corrections below hold for
  the notes above; the generated block and the page (version 94) carry them.
  1. *MJWarp at +15°.* "Climbs +15° in part" is the rule's label for 26 of 40 and must never stand without
     what the 26 are: none walks. At the line the pelvis is 0.24 m above the ground and moves at 1.95 m/s with
     the torso 31° forward (the checker's values; my 0.39 m and 1.55 m/s are medians over the last 2 s); 4 are
     terminated by a fall within 0.18 s after the line. The count is the position of the line: 40 / 38 / 35 /
     26 of 40 would pass a line at 3.5 / 4 / 4.5 / 5 m of slope. With the stop, MJWarp's 9 goals at +10° are of
     the same kind: with the stop it walked the 10° ramp in no trial.

     faster than commanded (I wrote "about 12 %"). "Without the stop the MPM policy still climbs +25°" is the
     committed rule's verdict over scored goals; it is quoted terrain by terrain: planar ramp, steady gait;
     slope tile, 36 of 40 of which 5 are crossings above 1.3 m/s (30 or 31 of 40 by the check's stricter
     count).

     +10°, none at +15°), for this condition only: planar ramp, beach sand, 0.50 m bed, default bed setting,
     1.0 m/s, 5 m of slope, the old code with the pair filtered. Not measured without the stop: the slope tile
     at +10° and +15°, the sand kept awake, a slope between 10° and 15°; the current code at +10° and +15° is
     running.

     (40 of 40 each), as on hardware; the earlier failure of the rigid-trained policy at 10° was produced by
     the stop. May not be said: that the simulation is validated by the hardware or reproduces it (one
     yes-or-no outcome on a slope no policy fails), or that MJWarp would fail at 15° on hardware. The hardware
     trial itself is not recorded in this project (surface, slope length, speed, number of trials).
  5. *Unit of repetition.* The five robots of a run each start on their own copy of the same settled sand, so
     the 40 trials of a cell come from 8 settled beds and are not independent. Goals per run for the counts
     strictly between 0 and 40 of these cells: MJWarp planar +10° with the stop 5, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0, 3 (9 of
     40); MJWarp planar +15° without the stop 5, 0, 4, 4, 2, 4, 4, 3 (26 of 40); over the 8 runs an exact sign
     test gives p = 0.016 for 9 → 40 and for 0 → 26 (the checker's values). No verdict rests on a cell at the
     edge: the statements rest on unanimous cells (0 of 40 against 40 of 40 walking trials, in every run).
  6. *The goal rule.* "Pelvis past the line without a termination" counts a fall through the line. From the
     first evaluation of the retrained policy on, every table carries the goal and the walking goal
     (definitions block, entry of 2026-11-26, 04:13; code `scripts/benchmarks/t3/walking_goal.py`, which
     reproduces the check's stricter counts in the ten cells I tried). The merged cells are not rescored.

     is unchanged (`logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/foot/slope_success_without_stop.png`, script

  - *D4 (scatter between submissions):* 26 against 26; D4 differs from the cell of 39 (p = 0.0003) and not from
    the cell of 26 (p = 1): by the reading, 39 against 26 is **"beyond the scatter between submissions"**.
  - *D3:* differs from the cell of 39 (p = 0.0001; −35 points, 95 % interval −51 to −18), not from the cell of
    26 (p = 1). *D2:* does not differ from the cell of 39 (p = 0.36); against the cell of 26 p = 0.0145 (+25
    points, +7 to +42), which is above the 0.01 of the rule. **By the letter of the reading: "not decided by
    these two cells"** (three of the four comparisons point to the tile grid, the fourth misses the threshold).
  - *What the five cells show, beyond the letter (not a rule fixed beforehand; wording and numbers corrected
    after Check 15).* The lane's end is not it: with the end far the count is 25 at r = 0.015 m (D3) and 39 at
    r = 0.115 m; with the end near it is 26, 26 and 36. The cells go with the remainder r of the lane's origin
    against the 0.2 m world tile grid: r = 0.015 m gives 26, 26, 25, r = 0.115 m gives 36 and 39. On the
    current scene that is 77 of 120 against 75 of 80 (+30 points, 95 % interval +19 to +39, p = 5.5e-7; this
    test counts the five robots of a run as independent). Over the 8 seeds the cells at r = 0.115 m have more
    goals in 8 of 8 (sign test p = 0.008); that is the test to quote. D2 minus D4 per run: 1, 0, 2, 0, 1, 3,
    1, 2. The course along the ramp separates them too: at r = 0.015 m the pelvis sinks to 0.26 to 0.28 m and
    the speed rises to 1.8 to 1.95 m/s; at r = 0.115 m 0.43 to 0.44 m and 1.37 to 1.44 m/s. (An earlier
    version of this bullet pooled 103 of 160, which included the 26 of the old code with the contact
    filtered, and gave the remainders as 0.013 and 0.113 m, which carried the feet's offset from the pelvis.)
  - *What r changes (Check 15, from the code and from running its two snapping kernels; my first reading,
    "the patch and the tile records out of step", is not what the code does).* With the tile cache on, the
    centre of the pool is snapped on a grid anchored at the lane's origin and then replaced by the nearest
    centre on the world tile grid, and that centre decides which particles are simulated. The pool always
    covers whole world tiles; what r moves is the place of the 1.2 m live window against the robot: 0.015 m
    behind the centred position at r = 0.015 m (the cells of 26, 26, 25) and 0.085 m ahead of it, uphill, at
    r = 0.115 m (the cells of 36 and 39). My own record of the live sand confirms the two places in a run of
    the task (−0.015 m and +0.085 m at every sample; note of 05:45). **That the window's place is the cause
    is not demonstrated;** the cells E0 to E9 and a control with a wider window test it.
  - *Which arrangement is the designed one (Check 15).* Every training environment has its origin on a tile
    centre of the 8 m terrain grid (r = 0, window centred on the robot within ±0.1 m). Every merged T3 cell

    window: MJWarp's limit between +10° and +15°, RFT at +30°, MJWarp at +10° with the stop, the MPM

  - *What "outside the awake box" means for the sand, from the code (read, not run; Newton `5ececcbe`,
    `particle_regions.py` `_filter_region_requests`, `particle_activity.py` `_classify_particles`,
    `mpm_solver_cfg.py`, `mpm_solver.py`; traced by a reading agent, the two kernels checked by me).* The
    preset has a strain planner and a box of 1.2 × 1.2 × 3.0 m around the pelvis with
    `keep_material_motion = True`. With a planner present the box does not keep the sand inside it awake; it
    limits where sand may be woken. Each patch of sand (2 voxels: 0.04 m in the evaluation, 0.08 m in
    training) is awake while something asks for it: its own motion, force or changing stress, 0.5 s of grace
    after the last of these, material moving into it, or a robot link moving near it. Outside the box only the
    last kind of request is thrown away. So sand outside the box **cannot be woken by a foot**, but sand that
    is already moving there (pushed out of the box, or sliding) stays simulated until it has been quiet for
    0.5 s. Once quiet it is one of two things: within one patch of awake sand it becomes "support", a fixed
    boundary at zero velocity that keeps its stress (infinite mass on the grid); further away it is left out
    of the solve entirely and neither a foot nor a grain feels it. The ring of the streaming pool is a
    different thing: its particles have zero mass, are put back on their sites every step and are always
    classed awake, so it is a fixed wall wherever it is. Nothing in the code ties the box's size to the
    window's; both are 1.2 m only because each is written so in the preset.
    *What that means for W1 and W2.* With the 1.6 m window the ring is 0.8 m from the window's centre and the
    box's edge 0.6 m from the pelvis. A foot beyond 0.6 m meets sand it cannot wake; sand set in motion
    inside the box can still flow outward to the ring. So the control did not give the feet more sand to
    step on: that much follows from the code. That it acted by moving the fixed wall away from the sand
    that the feet set in motion is **my inference, which no cell tests** (Check 16). The data in hand fit
    three readings alike: the wall ahead; the sleeping (quiet sand next to awake sand is a fixed boundary
    too, so sleeping makes walls of its own); and the time between a strip of sand becoming live at the
    front edge and a foot reaching it (0.4 s at s = −0.095 m, 0.8 s in W2). With the 1.2 m window the ring
    ahead is 0.48 to 0.68 m from the pelvis at the reference arrangement, inside the reach of waking (0.62 m)
    about two thirds of the time (I had written half). Not traced: whether the coupling writes forces on sand particles (that would keep
    patches awake), and how the stress kept by support sand enters the solve.
  - *Training against evaluation, settings of the sand (training from the W&B config of `aga5ovkp`,
    `logs/t3/wandb_compare/aga5ovkp_config.json`; evaluation from `eval_walk.py` and its result files).*

    | Setting | Training of the MPM policy (`aga5ovkp`) | T3 walking evaluation |
    |---|---|---|
    | Live window | 1.2 × 1.2 m | 1.2 × 1.2 m (wider only in the cells named) |
    | Ring around it | 0.2 m | 0.2 m |
    | Tile; pool | 0.2 m; 8 × 8 tiles | 0.2 m; 8 × 8 tiles |
    | Tile cache | **off** (`streaming.cache` is `None`, preset `solver_cfg_no_cache`): sand left behind is not remembered, and the window is snapped once, on the environment's own grid, so it has no shift whatever the origin | **on** (preset `solver_cfg_cache`): footprints are kept, and the window is snapped a second time onto the world tile grid, which gives the shift s |
    | Awake box | 1.2 × 1.2 × 3.0 m around the pelvis, material in motion outside stays awake | the same |
    | Strain planner | velocity 0.01 m/s, relative stress change 0.05, quiet time 0.5 s, stress filter 0.1 s; patch 0.08 m | the same values; patch 0.04 m |
    | Voxel | 0.04 m | 0.02 m |
    | Particles per cell edge | 1.25 | 1.25 |
    | Bed depth | 0.25 m | 0.50 m (0.35 and 0.25 m in the cells named) |
    | Origin remainder r, window shift s | origins on tile centres of the 8 m terrain grid: r = 0; no shift (no cache) | r = 0 on the slope and level tiles; r = 0.015 m, s = −0.015 m on the planar ramp |
    | Robots per GPU | 64 environments | 5 per run (4 or 2 in the cells named) |

    The tile cache is a difference between training and evaluation that I had not set beside the others
    before. It is why the window can be shifted in the evaluation at all.
    *The two retrains that are running (from T2's record, `outputs/T2.md`, "Resolved configuration of the two
    trainings against the logged configs of the runs they repeat", and its files
    `logs/t2/margin_fix/final/diff_vs_{aga5ovkp,cfbj8jpx}.json`, which I searched for the sand settings).*
    `j0scsgdy` (`g1-mpm-mixed-no-cache-fix-3`) repeats `aga5ovkp`: of the 47 entries that differ none is a
    setting of the sand, so it has the column "Training" above as it stands: 1.2 m window, **no tile cache**,
    1.2 m awake box, voxel 0.04 m, bed 0.25 m, r = 0 and no shift. `nofmls54` (`g1-mpm-mixed-cache-fix-2`)
    repeats `cfbj8jpx`, the run trained **with the tile cache**: of the 48 entries that differ none is a
    setting of the sand either. I have not opened `cfbj8jpx`'s own logged config; by its preset
    (`solver_cfg_cache`) it has the same window, ring, tile, awake box and planner, and with the training
    origins on tile centres (r = 0) the second snap leaves the window where the first put it (s = 0). So
    neither retrain sees a shifted window, both train inside a 1.2 m window, and the evaluation's preset
    (cache on) is the training condition of `nofmls54` and not of `j0scsgdy`.

    sand that is asleep under and ahead of the robot is not recorded in any of my runs.**
  - *Could the window effect be asleep sand seen from another side?* I cannot exclude it from the cells in.
    What speaks for the wall: the shift cells change only where the ring is, by centimetres, with the awake
    box and the planner untouched, and the result follows the distance of the ring ahead of the pelvis. What

    the sand awake changes this policy at +15° (0 against 23 of 40 on the tile with the stop). The cells B12,

  - *The four single-robot runs made for video (seeds 42 and 43, 1.2 m and 1.6 m window; looked at as stills
    from the side view, seed 42).* With the 1.2 m window the robot ends on its shin, lower leg flat on the
    sand, sand thrown up, and still passes the line (not a walking goal: 1.37 of the commanded speed). With
    the 1.6 m window the torso stays upright and the pelvis higher (0.49 to 0.50 m against 0.45 to 0.47 m),
    and it steps to the line at 1.10 of the commanded speed; its trailing shin still comes close to the sand
    in the frames I looked at. So "walks" for the 1.6 m window is by the walking-goal rule; from the stills I
    can say it is upright and stepping, not that the gait is that of level ground. One of the two 1.2 m runs
    (seed 43) is a walking goal by the rule (1.19 of the commanded speed), which the 5-robot cells never
    showed (0 of 200 with the window centred or behind centre).
  - *What differs with the number of robots per run (asked by the lead; from the code, read not run: Newton
    `5ececcbe`, traced by a reading agent, the three places named here checked by me).* Each robot has its own
    sand and no mass, momentum or stress passes between robots: the sparse grid holds each robot's sand as
    its own environment, with no faces between environments. But the solver's control is shared. (1) **One
    robot is another configuration:** `separate_worlds` is switched off when there is a single world
    (`solver_implicit_mpm.py`, `self._separate_worlds = config.separate_worlds and world_count > 1`), which
    changes the grid allocation and the convergence test. (2) **One iteration count for all robots:** the
    stress and contact iteration runs until every robot's residual is under the tolerance or 25 iterations
    are reached (`solve_rheology.py`, `update_batched_condition`: converged only if all batches are), so a
    hard contact under one robot gives the others more iterations. (3) **Warm starts are cleared for all
    robots when any robot's window takes in or gives up tiles:** the tile cache publishes one flag over all
    robots (`particle_cache_kernels.py`, `finalize_update`), and on it `CachedParticleStreaming` clears the
    contact warm start (the stored impulses between the links and the sand) and the collider history of every
    robot; the stress warm start is rebuilt from the particles every step in this configuration and is not
    touched (T1's finding, confirmed by Check 16; I had written "contact and stress")
    (`particle_streaming.py`, `_clear_reset_warmstarts(None, ...)`). With five robots that happens about
    five times as often for each of them as with one. (4) The start noise is drawn from one random stream for
    all robots of a run, so a robot's start depends on the seed and the number of robots. Not determined:
    how often the solve reaches its 25 iterations, and whether the rigid solver's shared contact pool is
    ever full. *What the data say (corrected after Check 16).* Four against five robots at the 1.2 m window on the L40S, V1
    against E0: I wrote "no difference shown" from the counts (31 and 31 goals, 0 and 0 walking goals). That
    is withdrawn. 32 of V1's 40 starts are starts of E0, and on them the pelvis rides 2.6 mm higher on the
    level approach with 4 robots per run (25 of 32 higher, p = 0.001); at s = +0.085 m, V2 against the cell
    of 39 and D2: +2.3 and +2.8 mm, walking goals 20 of 32 against 13 and 7. The two cells also differ in
    bundle, which is not controlled; the cells Q5, Q4, Q2 (entry of 07:40) separate the two. One robot per
    run is not answered by any cell in, and by (1) it is not the same solver.
    *How often the warm starts are cleared (asked by the lead; from the recorded pelvis positions, the
    window's steps recomputed as the solver takes them).* A robot's window changes tiles 4.5 to 4.7 times per
    second for RFT and MPM (level beach sand, planar +15°, tile +30°) and 6.1 times per second for MJWarp at
    planar +15° (5.4 to 7.7; it moves faster while it falls); 6 to 10 % of the changes are sideways steps. In
    a five-robot run that is 22 to 30 tile changes per second over the five robots, at 200 solver steps per
    second: about one solver step in seven to nine clears the contact warm start and the collider history of all
    five robots (for MJWarp at planar +15°: one in 6.1 to 7.6, Check 16), and 35 to 46 % of the control steps
    (50 per second) contain at least one. This is a lower bound: a particle drifting across the window's edge
    raises the same flag (T1). Whether the clearing changes the sand's response depends on whether the solve
    reaches its cap of 25 iterations, which no run of mine records. Alone, a robot
    would have it once in 33 to 44 solver steps. *The trainings.* One solver holds all sand environments of
    a GPU process (64 environments, half of them on sand by `sand_env_fraction = 0.5`: 32), so they share the
    iteration count. Without the tile cache (the setting of `aga5ovkp` and `j0scsgdy`) the pool counts
    recycled particles in one counter over all environments and clears the contact warm start of all of them
    when it is not zero (`particle_streaming.py`, `capture_if(self.mass_change_count, self._refresh_particle_mass)`),
    so the same holds there. With 32 walking environments at a few window steps per second each, that is of
    the order of a hundred clearings per second at 200 solver steps per second: an estimate, not measured in
    a training.
- *Cells without sleeping, and the window along the lane only (entries of 2026-11-26, 07:06 and 06:56;
  unchecked; in at 07:45).* MJWarp, planar ramp +15°, beach sand, 0.5 m bed, current scene, voxel 0.02 m,
  r = 0.015 m, 4 robots per run, seeds 41 to 50, 40 trials, identical starts across the five cells; pool
  `isaac-lab-l40s-03` (L40S). Script `window_series.py`, result
  `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/window_series/window_series.json`, cells `planar_win_{b12,b16,x16}`.

  | Cell | Live window | Sleeping | Goals of 40 | Walking goals | Pelvis / speed at 6.4 m | Pelvis on the level approach, paired by start |

  | V1 | 1.2 m | task preset | 31 | 0 | 0.33 m / 1.79 m/s | reference |
  | W1 | 1.6 m | task preset | 36 | 25 | 0.45 m / 1.21 m/s | |
  | X16 | 1.6 m along, 1.2 m across | task preset | 39 | 33 | 0.47 m / 1.16 m/s | |

  | B16 | 1.6 m | none | 40 | 40 | 0.54 m / 0.93 m/s | +27.8 mm against W1, 40 of 40; −1.7 mm against B12, lower in 36 of 40 (p = 1e-8) |

  - *Reading of 07:06.* B16 and B12 are within 4 walking goals (40 and 40): **"sleeping, not the wall"**. B12
    has at least 8 walking goals (40; V1 has 0): **"sleeping limits MJWarp at the 1.2 m window"**. (Check 17:
    once B12 was at 40 of 40 the reading could not return "the wall"; "sleeping, not the wall" is shown for
    this policy at this slope, where it no longer fails, and is not a general statement about the window.
    The cells of 10:28 ask it again where the policies are at the edge without sleeping.)
  - *What it says.* With every live particle simulated, the policy trained on rigid ground crosses the +15°
    ramp in every trial at 0.93 to 0.94 of the commanded speed with the pelvis at 0.54 m, with either
    window; RFT in the merged +15° cell has 0.55 m and 0.90 m/s. On level ground it rides 3 cm higher than
    on sand that sleeps, in every one of 40 paired trials: five times the whole effect of the window's
    placement. So "MJWarp does not walk +15°", and its dependence on where the window sits and on how wide
    it is, are found only with the task's sleeping. Without sleeping there is no failure at this slope for
    the window to act on; what remains of the window is 1.7 mm of pelvis height.
  - *X16 (06:56).* 33 walking goals. By the letter "count given without a sentence" (not within 4 of W1's
    25, not at most 4). Widening the window only along the lane does at least as well as widening it both
    ways.
  - *Limits.* One policy, one slope, one sand; L40S; 4 robots per run; RFT and the MPM checkpoint were not
    run without sleeping, so whether the order of the policies changes is not known; no slope above +15°
    without sleeping; "walks" is by the walking-goal rule (pelvis and speed as given), a side-view video of
    one run is being rendered. The cells A12 and A16 (sand in the box kept awake, sand outside it asleep,
    as in bed setting W) are submitted and not in.
  - *Cost (corrected after Check 17).* I wrote that the runs without sleeping took 0.85 s of wall time per
    control step against 0.92 s with it. That is withdrawn: the difference is the start-up of the first
    policy of a workflow spread over runs of different length. Like for like both arms take 0.53 to 0.57 s
    per control step (Check 17). Sleeping saves no time in this evaluation.
  - *What it touches.* Every merged T3 cell ran with the task's sleeping, except the cells of bed setting W
    (sand in the box kept awake, sand outside it asleep): every count, sinkage, sand-motion and pelvis
    number of the walking section is a number on sand that sleeps. T1's press test (force collapsing on
    asleep sand that wakes in patches) and my earlier 0 against 23 of 40 on the tile with the sand kept
    awake point the same way. The trainings of `aga5ovkp` and `j0scsgdy` run with the same sleeping. What
    is done with the merged statements and with the evaluation of the retrained policy is Junnosuke's
    decision.
  - *The sleeping preset: what it is, where it came from, who trains with it (asked by the lead; code and
    configs, no runs).* The evaluation's preset `solver_cfg_cache` (the task's default) sets
    `MPMSleepingCfg(strain=StrainActivationCfg(stress_filter_time=0.1), region=TrackedRegionActivationCfg(
    body_names="pelvis", size=(1.2, 1.2, 3.0)))`: sand is simulated patch by patch (2 voxels) only while it
    moves faster than 0.01 m/s, its stress changes by more than 5 %, a robot link moves near it inside the
    box, or for 0.5 s after the last of these; quiet sand next to simulated sand is a fixed boundary that
    keeps its stress, quiet sand further away is left out of the solve; outside the 1.2 m box a link cannot
    wake sand. Sand that enters the live window arrives able to sleep at once. In the history of the unified
    checkout the solver's sleeping was exposed on 2026-11-09 (`35bcc7720`), ported on 2026-11-18
    (`65093be1c`), and the streaming presets with it, `solver_cfg_cache` as the default, came with the
    snapshot of 2026-11-19 (`0013a0c2b`). *Trainings.* `aga5ovkp` and its repeat `j0scsgdy` use
    `solver_cfg_no_cache`: the same sleeping (same planner values, same 1.2 m box) and no tile cache.
    `nofmls54` repeats `cfbj8jpx` with `solver_cfg_cache`, which is the evaluation's preset; its patches are
    0.08 m (voxel 0.04 m) against 0.04 m in the evaluation. An earlier run in `logs/t3/wandb_compare`
    (`gk4b6ci0`) also has sleeping and the cache; the oldest (`eayrj79r`) has neither sleeping nor
    streaming. MJWarp and RFT were trained without particles, so their training has no counterpart of this
    setting: the sleeping sand is a ground only the MPM trainings have seen.
  - *Cost with and without sleeping (corrected after Check 17).* The numbers I gave here (0.92 against 0.85 s
    per control step on the pool; 60.8 s and 22.9 GB against 61.7 s and 21.3 GB locally) are withdrawn as a
    cost of sleeping: the pool numbers contain each workflow's start-up, and the local memory numbers are
    one unverified run each. Like for like the two arms take 0.53 to 0.57 s per control step, recording
    included (Check 17); GPU memory was not recorded in the cells. T1 measured the training task and found
    no difference either (its record, unchecked).

  The level cells are the slope tile at 0° in bed setting T (start on the settled surface), cells
  `planar_win_sl` and `planar_win_dl` under `ladder/ladder_poppy_seed_loose`; "level approach" there is the
  same stretch of the walk (pelvis between 0.3 and 1.5 m from the start).

  - *What the two arms differ in (Check 17).* The solver's sleeping, which is the planner and the box and
    also the solver's guarded stepping (`reuse_grid_topology = True`): the grid is built from the awake and
    support sand only, and a step whose grid exceeds its capacity is skipped with the particle state carried
    over. Whether a step was skipped is not recorded in any run. For MJWarp at +15° that path is controlled:
    A12 and A16 run it with all sand in the box awake and give what B12 and B16 give. For RFT, the MPM
    checkpoint and the other slopes it is not controlled separately and counts as part of "sleeping".
  - *MJWarp against RFT without sleeping (07:45), all four slopes in.* Walking goals 40 against 40 at +15°, 0
    against 40 at +20°, 0 against 39 at +25° and 0 against 33 at +30°: **"without sleeping the rigid-trained policy still fails where the RFT-trained one walks"** (the
    sentence needs one slope with MJWarp at most 4 and RFT at least 36; +20° is one). Against the MPM
    checkpoint: 40 against 40 at +15°, 0 against 40 at +20° and at +25°, 0 against 0 at +30°: the same
    sentence, by +20° and +25°. With the task's sleeping on the same starts MJWarp has no walking goal at
    any of the four slopes (0 against 40, 40, 40, 37 for RFT).
  - *RFT's counts at +25° and +30° sit on the lower speed bound (Check 17).* RFT climbs at 0.80 to 0.81 of the
    commanded speed at +25° and at 0.79 (sleeping) and 0.73 (no sleeping) at +30°, against a lower bound of
    0.7. With a bound of 0.8 its walking goals would be 38 → 16 at +25° and 13 → 1 at +30°; with 0.6, 40 → 39
    and 37 → 35. So "does not change the outcome" in the table is the committed word on a count that depends
    on the bound, not a statement that sleeping has no effect on RFT there. What does not depend on the
    bound: RFT reaches all 40 goals at +25° and +30° in both settings, and climbs +30° more slowly without
    sleeping (0.10 m/s slower at 5.4 m, p = 7e-7). Five of its seven non-walking goals at +30° without
    sleeping lose 82 to 183 mm of pelvis height before the line (three with sleeping).
  - *RFT against the MPM checkpoint at +30° without sleeping (07:45).* By the letter **"counts given"**: RFT
    has 40 goals and 33 walking goals, the MPM checkpoint 0 and 0; the sentence "RFT still climbs +30° where
    the MPM checkpoint falls" asked for 36 walking goals. With the task's sleeping on the same starts it is
    37 against 0 and the sentence holds. RFT's walking-goal count at +30° sits on the lower speed bound of
    0.7: its goals cross at a median 0.73 of the commanded speed without sleeping (0.69 to 0.83) and 0.79
    with (0.74 to 1.10); of the seven goals without sleeping that are not walking goals two are below the
    bound and five fail the sinking criterion. On goals the order at +30° is the same with and without
    sleeping.
  - *Level loose poppy seed (07:45, one cell, named as such).* Without sleeping MJWarp has 0 walking goals and
    RFT and the MPM checkpoint 40 each: **"without sleeping the rigid-trained policy still fails where the
    RFT-trained one walks"**, and where the MPM-trained one walks. MJWarp's failure on this sand is not the
    sleeping setting: it tips over within three seconds in every trial of either arm.
  - *What the four slopes say.* Without sleeping MJWarp walks +15° (40 of 40 here and in B12, on two bundles)
    and fails from +20° (no walking goal at +20°, +25° or +30°; 6, 0 and 0 goals); with the task's sleeping it has no
    walking goal from +15° on. RFT and the MPM checkpoint keep their outcomes at +20°, +25° and +30°. So with the 1.2 m window at its
    reference placement MJWarp's limit lies between +10° and +15° with the task's sleeping and between +15°
    and +20° without it (at +20° without sleeping the window has not been varied; cells of 10:28), and the
    separation from the RFT-trained policy stays from +20° and on loose poppy seed in both settings. At +15°
    the separation exists only with sleeping. The MPM checkpoint's 0 of 40 at +30° is a checkpoint outside
    its training condition (trained with the stop and with sleeping sand), not a result on training with
    particles.
  - *What sleeping changes for every policy (corrected after Check 17).* The pelvis rides 19 to 34 mm higher on
    level beach sand without sleeping, in 40 of 40 paired trials for each policy at each slope; on the slope
    42 to 61 mm higher for RFT, with one station that does not differ (4.4 m at +30°), and 18 to 56 mm for
    the MPM checkpoint; on level loose poppy seed 47 to 98 mm higher. **This is sinkage, not bed height**
    (Check 17): the two arms start from the same settled template (sand top equal within 0.2 mm at the first
    record), the sand top beside the robot is 2 to 3 mm lower without sleeping, and the feet sink 21 mm
    (RFT), 29 mm (MPM) and 32 mm (MJWarp) less on level beach sand and 35 to 38 mm less on level loose poppy
    seed. The beach-sand cells start with the drop, which lands the robot 5 to 7 cm deeper on sand that
    sleeps; part of the level-approach measure is the recovery from that. Why awake sand carries more is not
    shown, and neither setting has been compared with a full bed or with real sand. *How much of the sand is simulated with the task's sleeping (record
    `sand_sleep_share`, cells D20 and D30, medians over the samples of all trials):* under the pelvis 97 to
    99 % is awake for every policy; 0.3 to 0.6 m ahead of it 55 to 68 % is awake, 20 to 26 % is fixed
    support and 10 to 19 % is left out; over the whole window about 65 %, 12 % and 22 %. (I had added that on the
    +30° slope the awake share ahead drops to 35 to 39 % for the two policies that fall there; Check 17 does
    not reproduce that and finds 45 and 56 %. It is withdrawn.) The sinkage, pelvis-height and sand-motion numbers of the walking section are
    numbers of the sleeping setting.
  - *A12 (entry of 07:06), the sand in the box kept awake and the sand outside it asleep, 1.2 m window,
    MJWarp at +15°:* 40 of 40 goals, 40 walking goals, pelvis 0.54 m at 0.95 m/s at 6.4 m: the same as with
    no sleeping at all (B12). So at +15° it is the sleeping of settled sand inside the box (the strain
    planner) that makes the difference, not the sleeping outside the box. **A16** (the same with the 1.6 m
    window): 40 of 40 goals, 40 walking goals, 0.53 m at 0.92 m/s. By the reading of 07:06: **"with the sand
    in the box kept awake the window does not matter"** (40 against 40). On the level approach the pelvis is
    6.9 mm lower with the wider window in all 40 paired trials (without any sleeping: 1.7 mm lower), so a
    small effect of the window's size remains on the continuous measure.
  - *Limits.* L40S; 4 robots per run; one sand; the planar ramp only; the 1.2 m window only; none of these
    cells seen on video except one run of MJWarp at +15° without sleeping.

<!-- T3-grid-for-T1:start -->
### For T1: the streaming window against the robot, its placement and its size (text of Check 16, section C5; replaces the earlier versions of this block)

**The streaming window against the robot: its placement and its size (from T3's walking evaluation,
`checks/T3.md` Checks 15 and 16).** *Placement, from the code.* With the tile cache on, the pool's centre is
computed twice per step: `update_patch_center` snaps the anchor body to a 0.2 m grid anchored at the
environment's origin, and `compute_tile_begins` replaces that centre by the nearest centre on the world tile
grid; the replaced centre decides which particles are simulated. With r the remainder of the environment's
origin against the tile, the window is shifted against the anchor by s = −r for r below half a tile and by one
tile minus r above it; it steps at the same anchor positions for every r. Without the cache there is one snap
and no shift. Every training environment has r = 0 (origins on tile centres of the 8 m terrain grid). In 16
cells of the walking evaluation the measured centre of the window equals the formula at every sample. *Shown,
placement.* Policy trained on rigid ground, planar +15 degree ramp, beach sand, 0.02 m voxel, 1.2 m window with
a 0.2 m ring, 40 trials per cell with identical starts in the lane's frame, 5 robots per run, one GPU pool:
goals of 40 rise with s from 0 at s = −0.095 m through 20 to 26 at −0.015 m and 28 at +0.005 m to 36 to 39 at
+0.085 and +0.095 m; two origins 0.01 m apart on either side of r = 0.1 m give 0 and 38; the pelvis height on
the level approach follows s from −8.2 to +5.6 mm. By a reading committed before the cells were run the result
follows the window's shift and not a 0.04 m period, the voxel phase or the lane's place on the tile grid.
*Shown, size.* On identical starts (4 robots per run) the same policy has 0 walking goals of 40 with the 1.2 m
window and 25 with a 1.6 m window at s = −0.015 m (31 and 36 goals), and 22 and 35 at s = +0.085 m; a walking
goal is a goal at 0.7 to 1.3 times the commanded speed without sinking. With the 1.6 m window the placement no
longer changes the pelvis height on level ground (+0.8 mm, p = 0.25) and still changes the climb (pelvis 40 mm
higher at 5.4 m, p = 2e-5). The RFT-trained policy reaches 40 of 40 at both placements of the 1.2 m window; it
was not run with the wider one. *From the code, bearing on the cause.* Ring particles have zero mass and enter
the grid with infinite mass, so the ring is a boundary of zero velocity. With the task's sleeping the 1.2 m box
around the pelvis does not keep sand awake; it limits where the robot's links may wake sand (0.62 m from the
pelvis). Sand in motion stays simulated anywhere in the window until it has been quiet for 0.5 s, and quiet sand
next to awake sand is itself a boundary of zero velocity. Sand enters the window at its front edge in the
template's settled state and may sleep at once. *Not shown.* The cause (the ring ahead, the sleeping, or the
time between sand entering the window and a foot reaching it). Which edge acts: the distances of simulated sand
ahead of and behind the pelvis add up to a constant in the 1.2 m series; walking goals rise with the distance
ahead over all cells (0.40 to 0.78 m) and are not ordered by the distance behind; the pelvis height on level
ground is ordered by neither alone. The window size at which the result stops changing (1.6 m is the widest
run). Any other policy, slope or sand, and the 0.04 m voxel of the trainings. Any effect on the memory or step
times of this section. A box test by T1 (one run per cell, box 6 cm deep, 0.55 m or more from the ring) found no
change in the drag across four origins, which does not test a body whose contacts come near the ring. *Robots in
one run are not independent in the solver's control.* No sand passes between environments. With one environment
`separate_worlds` is switched off (another grid allocation and stopping rule). With several, the stress and
contact iteration stops for all of them when every environment has converged or at the cap of 25 iterations, and
a tile change in any environment (with the cache) or any particle changing between ring and window (without it)
discards the contact warm start of every environment: one solver step in six to nine in the 5-robot evaluation,
of the order of every second step in a training with 32 sand environments (estimate). On 32 identical starts the
pelvis rides 2.6 mm higher on the level approach with 4 robots per run than with 5 (p = 0.001; the two cells
also differ in their code bundle). Whether the cap of 25 is reached in these runs is not recorded. *For this
section it means:* the memory and step-time results stand. That a robot in the moving window behaves as on an
unbounded bed is not shown, and for the 1.2 m window it fails in this test: at the limit of a policy the outcome
of a walking task depends on the window's size, on a shift of its placement by centimetres and on how many
environments share the solver. A statement that streaming does not change the outcome needs a window-size series
that converges for the task, and the conditions of every benchmark should state the window, r and the number of
environments.

Data: `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/planar_{asset,hold,diag2,diag3,diag4}`, `planar_shift<k>` (pool
`isaac-lab-l40-07`), `planar_shiftS<k>` (pool `isaac-lab-l40s-03`) and `planar_window_{v1,v2,w1,w2}`, each with
`slope/beach_sand_+15/mjwarp.{json,npz}`, in the unified checkout; scripts `shift_cells.py`, `window_control.py`.
The lane's origin is −L/2 plus the lane before the start (`eval_walk.py --behind`); the window's size is
`--window`. Cells with wider and longer windows, without sleeping, and with 1, 2, 4 and 5 robots per run are
running (definitions block, entries of 2026-11-26, 06:56 to 07:40).
<!-- T3-grid-for-T1:end -->
- *A condition of every T3 walking evaluation that was not stated before: the sand is solved on a 0.02 m grid,
  the MPM policy was trained on a 0.04 m grid (found 2026-11-26 while answering the lead).* All 1,923 result
  files under `logs/t3/final_1` record `voxel_m = 0.02` and 1.25 particles per cell edge; 0.02 m is the
  default of `eval_walk.py --voxel` and no T3 walking run sets another value. The saved training
  configuration of the MPM policy `aga5ovkp` (`logs/t3/wandb_compare/aga5ovkp_config.json`) has
  `voxel_size = 0.04` for the sand, the terrain and the solver, with the same 1.25 particles per cell edge,
  0.25 m of sand and 0.2 m tiles; the current walking scene file has 0.04 as well (the snapshot of 2026-11-19
  had 0.03). So the evaluation's MPM grid is twice as fine as the training's and its sand has eight times as
  many particles per volume; the sand tile is 10 voxels in the evaluation and 5 in the training. I find no
  recorded decision that the evaluation should differ from the training: the value goes back to the first
  version of the walking test, whose bed check compared 0.02, 0.03 and 0.04 m on level poppy seed. What it
  means: every sinkage, sand-motion and success number of T3 is for a finer sand than any policy was trained
  on (the baselines were not trained on MPM sand at all); whether the results hold at 0.04 m is not measured
  by the merged cells. The towing evaluation of T2 uses 0.04 m. This is stated in the generated conditions
  text from now on; a reader should not assume the same sand in training and test.
<!-- T3-shin-notes:end -->

## Final-checkpoint rerun of P1 (2026-11-24): what was run, what failed, what is open

Status: measured in this session on OSMO pool `isaac-lab-l40-07` (L40), not yet independently checked. The
results themselves are in the blocks "P1-1 terrain geometry" (slopes, bed depth, 40-trial steep slopes,
provenance), "P1-1 terrain geometry, explanation" (sand under the foot on slopes) and "P1-2 terrain material"
(density-friction grid, material ladder with the behaviour comparison). This block records the runs and what
does not fit on a slide.

**Policies.** MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp`, each at iteration 19,999. `aga5ovkp`: W&B state
"finished" (checked 02:24), last checkpoint `model_19999.pt`, sha256 `17b8220cd47d…`. The earlier MPM policy
`eayrj79r` is not shown anywhere any more; its data stay under `logs/t3/eval_5` and `logs/t3/flow_1`.

**Code.** Unified worktree, Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with 72 uncommitted entries (`scripts/benchmarks/t3/` is
untracked), Newton `48231a98` clean, Warp `3d0f4e5b` clean. Another session committed to Isaac Lab and Newton at
02:21 (towing tasks; particle-cache store size), so this is not the code of the first pass. Bundle 1
(`bundles/bd2f5052f974fca8`, 02:25) serves every workflow except ladder C; bundle 2 (`bundles/ee1a9dad58dcb623`,
04:41, Isaac Lab with 76 uncommitted entries) adds the option `eval_walk.py --spawn_z` and serves ladder C. Each
result file records commit, uncommitted count, bundle, workflow, seed, command line and GPU; the list per
evaluation root is in the provenance part of the geometry block. Scripts: `scripts/benchmarks/t3/` (new in this
rerun: `merge_trials.py`, `depth_compare.py`, `material_behaviour.py`, `provenance.py`; changed: `eval_walk.py`,
`eval_rigid.py`, `base_clearance.py`, `osmo_workflow.py`, `yielding_slides.py`, `slope_flow_slides.py`,
`site_slides.py`, `paper_figures.py`, `tow_force_figure.py`). Data: `logs/t3/final_1/` (roots `d25`, `d35`,
`d50`, `steep35`, `steep50`, `ladder35/50/65` = ladder C, `ladderB35/50/65`, `ladderA35/50/65`, `rigid_all`),
raw downloads `logs/t3/flow_dl/t3-final-*`.

**Workflows** (all `t3-final-<name>-1-1`; three policies in each; 41 workflows in this table. My message to the lead said 43, which was a miscount).

**Failures and what was done.** Every failure was the same: the sand template did not settle to below 0.03 m/s
in the 2.5 s allowed (0.030–0.044 m/s). It hit the 500 kg/m³ sand on the 0.50 m bed and, by a hair
(0.0304–0.0313 m/s), dense sand and both poppy seeds on some beds of the ladder. Fix: 6 s settling for those
cells of the material axis and for the whole ladder (rerun once, as agreed for overnight runs; no second
failure). 0.50 m and 0.65 m runs use 5 and 4 trials per run because 10 do not fit in the memory of one L40
(local measurement: 14.6 GB for 2 trials at 0.50 m against 10.8 GB at 0.35 m; the local GPU was shared, so this
is rough).

**The four issues of `checks/T3.md`.**

1. *Explanation and headline on different beds*: closed. The headline sweep and the explanation (sand motion,
   sole angle, failure types, close-up, schematic) are both from the 0.50 m bed, all three policies tracked in
   the same workflows.
2. *MPM against RFT rests on an invalid cell*: closed. On the 0.50 m bed +30° is clear of the base (0 of 40 RFT
   trials and 1 of 40 MPM trials within 2 cm) and MPM reaches 40 of 40 against 13 of 40 (Fisher p = 2e-11). At
   +25° the two are not separated (40 against 39 of 40) and the slides say so.
3. *The 2 cm rule is not validated*: closed by dropping the rule. It was tested on 75 cells between the 0.35 and
   0.50 m beds with tolerances fixed beforehand and does not pick out the cells that change; results are quoted
   from the deepest bed instead. Open remainder: MJWarp's results keep getting worse with depth, and loose poppy
   seed still changes between 0.50 and 0.65 m.
4. *Coverage and provenance*: closed for MPM on the material axis and on the grid, for provenance in the result
   files and blocks, for the training budget (W&B configs) and for the rigid-ground reference (rerun on the
   cluster with raw data). Not closed: one training seed and one checkpoint per policy; trial start states on the
   0.50 and 0.65 m beds are not the same as on the shallower beds.

Small flaws of the check: base height now read under each sole corner; "every trial" wording replaced by counts.

| Peak normal load [body weights] | 1.29 / 1.28 | 1.86 / 1.90 | 1.30 / 1.26 | 2.06 / 2.02 | 1.66 / 1.80 | 2.09 / 2.07 |
| Peak tangential load [body weights] | 0.75 / 0.78 | 1.25 / 1.25 | 0.94 / 0.94 | 1.52 / 1.52 | 1.29 / 1.45 | 1.85 / 1.86 |
| Tangential / normal load, mean | 0.48 / 0.49 | 0.57 / 0.58 | 0.62 / 0.62 | 0.74 / 0.73 | 0.84 / 0.87 | 0.95 / 0.94 |
| Pelvis leans forward [deg] | 3.6 / 3.7 | 4.9 / 5.1 | 4.4 / 4.4 | 6.2 / 6.7 | 6.5 / 6.6 | 8.6 / 8.7 |
| Pelvis ahead of the loaded foot [cm] | 3.0 / 3.1 | 6.9 / 7.0 | 5.0 / 4.8 | 8.4 / 8.5 | 8.9 / 8.6 | 10.1 / 10.2 |

- *"MPM makes the sand yield less"*: supported by the rule fixed beforehand. On the 0.35 m bed, per seed, the
  sand under the sole moves 2.06–2.20 cm (MPM) against 3.26–3.30 cm (RFT) at +20° and 3.44–3.57 against
  4.33–4.63 cm at +25°: lower in every seed, by about 1 cm, which is more than RFT's range between seeds
  (0.04 and 0.30 cm).
- Peak load, corrected after the recheck (`checks/T3.md`, N6): the peak loads in the table are taken from the
  first 50 Hz sample above 20 N on, which leaves out the up to 15 ms in which the foot lands. With that interval
  included RFT's median peak normal load is 1.52–1.57 body weights at +25° and 2.20–2.40 at +30°, as high as or
  higher than MPM's 2.04–2.10. So "MPM pushes harder than RFT" holds at +20° only (1.9 against 1.3 body
  weights) and is not a general statement.
- What differs in the body: MPM leans further forward and carries the pelvis 3–4 cm further ahead of the loaded
  foot at +20° and +25°. RFT's failures are falls forward (30 of 30 on 0.35 m; 25 of 27 on 0.50 m).
- This is a description. Which of these differences keeps MPM up at +30° is not established.

**Three things about the simulated bed that this rerun found** (details in the definitions block and in the
material block): the settling time changes the bed a little (6 s: surface about 1 cm lower, feet sink 10–20 %
less); the default start drops the robot 4 to 16 cm onto the settled surface, more on deeper beds, which alone
decides RFT's result on loose poppy seed on the 0.65 m bed (0 of 12 with the drop, 9 of 12 without); and on
some deep beds the settled sand does not yield under the feet (dense sand on 0.50 m for MJWarp and on 0.65 m
for every policy, beach sand on 0.65 m in 22 of 36 trials), cause not established.

**Paper figures written** (new files in the paper's `figures/`, nothing overwritten, no `.tex` touched):
`t3_slope_final.pdf` (uphill, 0.50 m bed), `t3_slope_full_final.pdf` (−30° to +30°), `t3_material_final.pdf`
(3D mesh over the density-friction grid, 0.35 m bed), `t3_yielding_final.pdf` (slope and mesh in one figure),
`t3_material_ladder_final.pdf` (material ladder on level ground, 0.50 m bed) and `t3_tow_final.pdf` (towing at
equal cube speed). Drafts and 2D alternatives of the mesh: `logs/t3/paper_fig_final/`.

**Open.** No videos of the final rerun. The density-friction grid was not repeated on the 0.50 m bed or with
the start on the settled surface. The slope sweep was not repeated with the start on the settled surface or
with 6 s settling. The cause of the sand that does not yield is not known.

### After the recheck of the final rerun (2026-11-24, runs unchecked)

The recheck (`checks/T3.md`, "rerun" sections) reproduced every number and raised the points N1 to N11. What was
done about them, in the order the lead asked for:

1. *What makes the sand stop yielding (N1).* Not sleeping alone: the bed keeps losing height while it is awake,
   and sleeping freezes it in whatever state it has. Diagnostic table and reading in the definitions block
   ("two bed configurations"). The ladder with 6 s settling (ladders B and C above) is withdrawn as a result.
   The post-hoc threshold for "sand that does not yield" is no longer used on any figure.
2. *One setting per comparison (N2, N4).* The material part is now on one bed (0.50 m), one start (on the settled
   surface, measured by each task before its trials) and one velocity-error definition (from 1.0 s, trials that
   reach the goal), run in two bed settings, T (2.5 s settling, the task's sleeping) and W (12 s settling, all
   sand near the robot awake). Statements are made only where T and W agree. The first material axis is no
   longer shown.
3. *Grid settled as beach sand (N8).* The density-friction grid was rerun with every sand generated and settled as
   itself (`osmo_workflow.py --sand_grid`), in T and in W. The 3D mesh figures come from the T run; sands whose
   bed collapses while settling are marked instead of the 2 cm rule (N3 of the earlier list, item 6).
4. *Slopes without the start drop (N3).* Steep slopes on the 0.50 m bed, 40 trials per policy, started on the
   settled surface with 2.5 s and with 6 s settling, and in setting W. Goals at +30° (MJWarp / RFT / MPM): 0 / 13
   / 40 with the default start; 0 / 9 / 40 and 0 / 18 / 40 on the surface; 0 / 1 / 40 in W. MPM against RFT is
   separated at +30° in every setting (p from 8e-9 to 8e-22) and not at +25° (39 or 40 against 40). The

   (corrected above).
6. *Markers and count (N3 remainder, N11).* The 2 cm markers are off the grid figure; 41 workflows were in the

7. *Wording (lead's summary).* The P1-2 slide now says: MPM against MJWarp is supported; MPM against RFT is not

entries, Newton `48231a98`; it adds `eval_walk.py --sleep` to bundle 2): `t3-final-surf50-s41…48-1-1` and
`t3-final-surf50six-s41…48-1-1` (steep slopes on the surface), `t3-final-steepW-s41…48-1-1`,
`t3-final-ladderT-s41/42-1-1`, `t3-final-ladderW-s41/42-1-1`, `t3-final-gridT-1-1`, `t3-final-gridW-1-1`. All
completed except grid cells with friction 0.1 (T: 250 and 500 kg/m³ did not settle to 0.06 m/s; W: six of seven
densities collapsed onto the floor), which are reported as collapsed beds. Data roots under

`logs/t3/settle_diag/` and `logs/t3/settle_probe/`.

**Low-friction beds and the empty-cell explanation (lead's question).** In the grid the settled surface depends on
the friction only and is the same for every density: 8.3, 11.6, 15.0, 20.8, 25.1 and 30.1 cm below the generated
level for friction 0.9, 0.7, 0.5, 0.3, 0.2 and 0.1 after 2.5 s (10.0, 13.7, 18.1, 27.0, 32.7 and 37.9 cm after
12 s). Empty grid cells at the sampling density (T1's finding, 12 to 14 % of the cells) would give the same loss
for every friction, about 6 to 7 cm of a 50 cm bed if filling them removed that share of the height. So the
empty cells can account for most of the loss at friction 0.9, not for the three to four times larger loss at low
friction. A second, friction-dependent cause is needed; the bed spreading sideways toward its angle of repose
is a candidate that fits "friction only, not density", and it is not tested.

each sand settled as itself, collapsed beds marked), `t3_yielding_final2.pdf` (slope and that mesh),
`t3_material_ladder_final2.pdf` (ladder in setting T).

### Sand flow under the foot across materials (2026-11-24, checked: pass with caveats)

Requested by Junnosuke through the lead; measures and criterion are in the definitions block (three entries of
this afternoon and one correction after the check). The result, its tables and the test are in the block "P1-2
terrain material", part "Sand flow under the foot"; on the slides it is the third figure of P1-2. Check 4 in
`checks/T3.md` reproduced every number from the raw downloads and asked for the rewording below; the values as
volume of the settled bed and the values without the side region taken off were added after it and are not
checked again.

- *Runs.* The material ladder repeated in settings W and T with a tracker that compares every footprint before
  and after (`foot_sand.py`: `column_change`, `region_budget`, arrays `stance_col_*`): `t3-final-flow2W-s41/42-1-1`
  and `t3-final-flow2T-s41/42-1-1` (bundle `bundles/1a9aaf78bfb030a3`, Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with 76 uncommitted
  entries, Newton `48231a98`), used for every number; `t3-final-flowW/T-s41/42-1-1` (bundle
  `bundles/c9caf3e1c88bb919`), submitted minutes earlier without the region budget, give the same column values.
  All eight completed. Data roots `logs/t3/final_1/flow2W`, `flow2T`, `flowW`, `flowT`; analysis
  `scripts/benchmarks/t3/material_flow.py`. A trial is one start of a robot; each robot starts on its own
  copy of the same settled sand; 10 trials per policy and ground.
- *First attempt, no result.* The particle boxes saved by the earlier ladder runs hold 0 to 3 footprints per
  cell that the foot has already left (sand is simulated only within 0.8 m of the pelvis), so the split could
  not be taken from them.
- *Answer.* More sand leaves the footprint column on softer ground, for every policy, in both settings (W: 0.16
  to 0.18 L on dense sand, 1.33 to 1.53 L on loose poppy seed, litres at nominal density; as volume of the
  settled bed 0.12 to 0.13 and 0.92 to 1.03 L). It does not come up beside the foot. In the region around the
  footprint in setting W, 65 to 88 % of what sinks is compaction (65–83 % with the region to the side taken
  off, 72–84 % without, 80–88 % with the flow as volume of the settled bed); this does not hold for the column
  under the sole (flow is 37–70 % of the rut as volume of the settled bed) and not in setting T as reported.
  Whether degradation follows flow is **not shown, and not separable from sinkage**: MPM does not degrade, RFT's
  error is flat, and for MJWarp the rank correlation is 0.70 in W over 5 grounds and 1.00 in T over 4 grounds
  (0.8 in both was required), with the same values for sinkage on the same grounds; the grounds where MJWarp
  falls in every trial have no footprint and enter neither this test nor the test between policies.
- *Supported, narrower.* On the softest ground where it still steps (light sand in W: 15 footprints from 7
  trials; packed poppy seed in T: 129 footprints) MJWarp moves about a third to a half more sand out of the
  footprint column and sinks 40 to 80 % deeper than RFT and MPM. On packed poppy seed in W it moves 63–69 % more
  out of the column without failing (10 of 10, 0.07 m/s) and not more out of the region. On dense and beach
  sand the three policies move the same sand while MJWarp and RFT track 0.05 to 0.08 m/s worse than MPM, so flow
  does not account for that gap.
- *Method caveats from the check*, all stated with the result: the unit (above); the region to the side is not
  undisturbed (W: sinks 0.44–0.85 L as the robot passes; T: gains 0.1–0.6 L of sand, so taking it off overstates
  T's flow); the budget closes by construction; the criterion was first committed after the results.
- *While these ran the disk filled up* (1.1 GB free at 13:07); one bundle failed on it and the bundles and the
  first downloads were staged in `/dev/shm` until Junnosuke freed space. Nothing was deleted by this session;
  the three policy checkpoints were confirmed present with unchanged hashes afterwards.
- *Paper figure*: `t3_material_flow_final2.pdf` (new file; same plot, panel titles now carry the unit and "side
  region taken off"). `t3_material_flow_final.pdf` is superseded and left in place.

### How forces are recorded: towing, dragged cube, feet (2026-11-24, unchecked)

Asked by the lead after T1 found that its box probe reads the sand force only at the last of the four physics
steps of a control step, where it is lowest. Read from the code and the saved files; no new runs.

- *Towing (tether tension and ground force on the cube): not affected.* The tension is the held value that
  acted: `Tether.apply()` computes the rope force once per control step and holds it for all four physics steps,
  and that value is logged. The ground force on the cube is never read from the sand solver: it is mass times
  acceleration (cube velocity at the control samples, 0.2 s average) minus the rope force minus weight
  (`tow_report.box_ground_force`), so it is the mean over the physics steps by construction, on MPM sand, RFT
  sand and rigid ground alike. The tow-in-a-channel runs use the same two records.
- *Dragged level cube (block "A. Controlled box test"): affected in kind, probably 5 to 10 % low, not proven.*
  Details are with that test.
- *Foot loads on MPM sand.* The control-rate record is read after the last physics step. The runs with the
  footprint tracker also hold the force after every physics step, so the difference was measured on the final
  rerun (54 files): vertical load in stance, last step against the mean of the four, 0.98 to 1.005 in every
  cell; share of time a foot is loaded the same within 0.01. Stance detection, sinkage and goals are not
  affected, and the slope explanation's load time, loading rate and peak load already use the physics-rate
  record. The horizontal load read at the last step is 0.86 to 1.02 of the mean of the four (MJWarp policy 0.96
  to 1.02, RFT 0.86 to 1.01, MPM 0.86 to 0.99).
- *Two columns recomputed.* "Peak tangential load" and "tangential / normal" of the material ladder are now
  taken from every physics step (`material_behaviour.py`; hard ground keeps the contact sensor's control-rate
  values). The peak rises by 4 to 63 % (largest for RFT: 0.19 to 0.31 body weights on light sand in setting W),
  the ratio changes by −7 to +13 %. The earlier values are kept in the table beside the new ones. No quantity
  passes the explanation rule before or after, and no statement rested on these two columns. Peak normal load
  and loading rate of that table, and the peak tangential load of the steep-slope comparison
  (`steep_compare.py`), are still read once per control step.

Check 5 in `checks/T3.md` reproduced every number of the slope explanation from the raw downloads and rejected

## Values for the paper's walking table, with the stance foot velocity (2026-11-24, unchecked)

Status: computed in this session from the existing result files, no new runs, not independently checked.
Requested by Junnosuke through the lead for `tab:t3_walk`. Files for the paper session:
[site/T3/data/t3_walk_table.csv](../site/T3/data/t3_walk_table.csv) (every number, both material settings) and
[site/T3/data/t3_walk_table_rows.tex](../site/T3/data/t3_walk_table_rows.tex) (rows in the table's layout).
Script: `scripts/benchmarks/t3/walk_table.py` (unified worktree); output `logs/t3/final_1/walk_table/`.

**Stance foot velocity, definition.** Horizontal speed of the centre of the sole between two consecutive control
samples (50 Hz) in both of which that foot carries more than 20 N, averaged over all such sample pairs of both
feet, from 1.0 s on (on a slope: from 0.3 m past the edge of the start platform), then averaged over the
trials that reach the goal. This is exactly the "contact foot velocity" panel of the earlier slope and
material figures (`foot_slip_m_s` of `summarize_walk.py`; the slope values reproduce `geometry.json`). One
sentence for the caption: "Stance foot velocity is the mean horizontal speed of the sole centre over the
control samples in which the foot carries more than 20 N."

**It is not the bearing-point speed of the fifth check.** That quantity follows the lowest of heel, middle and
toe along the surface from the first to the last sample above 20 N of each stance, over all trials with at
least three stances. It is given in the last column for comparison (0.54 / 0.11 / 0.16 m/s on level beach sand,
0.84 and 0.70 m/s for RFT and MPM at +30°, as the check confirmed). The two agree within 0.05 m/s on most
cells and differ where the sole pitches during stance (RFT at +30°: 0.56 against 0.84 m/s).

**Conventions, the same as for e_v and step time in the table.** Mean ± standard deviation over the trials
that reach the goal, number of trials in brackets; a dash where no trial reaches the goal. A trial is one
start of a robot, each robot on its own copy of the same settled sand (position ±0.1 m, yaw ±0.1 rad, joints ±10 %); 10 trials per
cell (seeds 41 and 42), 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s command, final checkpoints. Step time here is one over the step
frequency counted from the ankle height, for every row.

**Bed settings.** Slopes: the setting of the slope sweep (default start, which drops the robot onto the settled
surface; 2.5 s settling; the task's sleeping). Materials: setting T (start on the settled surface, 2.5 s
settling, the task's sleeping), the one the table's SR and e_v values come from. Hard ground is rigid contact
without sand.

**Slopes, beach sand (1800 kg/m³, 0.80), default setting.**
